| Optimizer CPU hours | 82.80 h | process CPU time in the trial loops of the same slices, excluding initialization and recovery |
| Interrupted intervals | twenty-six session restarts of about 30 s each | session supervisor log |

The records contain 787 distinct completed guarded runs, 684 of them successful, and 1,437 completed unguarded calculations from a ledger of their start and end times. The failed guarded run of the fourth slice contributes its 971 s to the recorded compute only. The interval of 936 s that the driver recorded for that interruption is not counted as lost or as useful work: it runs from the start of the last committed trial to the startup of the next worker, and only 89.4 s of it lie between the last commit and the kill (section 7).

A separate statistic, reproduced independently, uses only the timestamps of the transcripts of all working, helper and consultation sessions, not their content. After sorting the timestamps within each transcript and taking the union of intervals between adjacent records at most 610 seconds apart, recorded session timestamp coverage leaves 1.951 seconds uncovered in the 48 hours 55 minutes elapsed at the 04:00:00Z cut; the coverage is 48.12 hours with a 120-second threshold. These statistics include waiting and polling and do not measure useful work or active investigator time. At the 08:00:00Z time-only cut on 2026-10-26, the same timestamp statistic, reconstructed independently from the 143 hashed source-file prefixes recorded by the coordinating session, covers 52.914598888889 h of the 52.916666666667 h window when the maximum adjacent sorted gap is 610 s, leaving exactly 7.444 s uncovered. The calculation first restricts each prefix to timestamps in the campaign window, sorts them within that prefix, includes adjacent pairs at most 610 s apart, and takes their union across prefixes. With thresholds of 300, 120 and 60 s, the corresponding coverage is 52.907721111111, 52.115683055556 and 49.604612500000 h. These values refer to the recorded prefixes; later appends, including records with pre-cut timestamps, are excluded. The statistic uses timestamps only, includes waiting and polling, and does not certify useful research duration, continuous solver progress or active investigator time. Quantitative verification of 48 h of genuine research or useful computation therefore remains open.

At the earlier cutoffs the same quantities, also reproduced independently, were: at 2026-10-25T03:23:19Z 24.31 h, 24.31 h, 20.77 h, 31.38 h, 1.88 h and 3.80 h; at 05:40:21Z 26.59 h, 26.59 h, 22.97 h, 34.10 h, 2.69 h and 7.08 h; at 06:39:10Z 27.57 h, 27.57 h, 23.73 h, 35.07 h, 3.28 h and 9.56 h; at 07:38:33Z 28.56 h, 28.56 h, 24.94 h, 36.84 h, 3.71 h and 11.27 h; at 08:38:49Z 29.56 h, 29.56 h, 26.10 h, 38.43 h, 4.86 h and 16.17 h; at 09:38:09Z 30.55 h, 30.55 h, 27.03 h, 39.62 h, 4.86 h and 16.17 h; at 10:38:13Z 31.55 h, 31.55 h, 28.10 h, 41.02 h, 5.93 h and 20.90 h; at 11:38:51Z 32.56 h, 32.56 h, 29.14 h, 42.40 h, 6.99 h and 25.61 h; at 12:38:03Z 33.55 h, 33.55 h, 29.99 h, 43.69 h, 6.99 h and 25.61 h; at 13:38:11Z 34.55 h, 34.55 h, 31.05 h, 45.66 h, 8.02 h and 30.04 h; at 14:51:00Z 35.77 h, 35.77 h, 32.05 h, 47.45 h, 9.08 h and 34.75 h; at 16:30:00Z 37.42 h, 37.42 h, 33.96 h, 49.68 h, 10.13 h and 39.44 h; at 18:00:00Z 38.92 h, 38.92 h, 35.15 h, 51.16 h, 11.28 h and 44.23 h; at 20:00:00Z 40.92 h, 40.92 h, 37.36 h, 53.84 h, 13.50 h and 54.06 h; at 2026-10-26T00:00:00Z 44.92 h, 44.92 h, 40.82 h, 57.87 h, 16.90 h and 68.95 h.

The records then contained 873 distinct completed guarded runs, 754 of them successful, and 1,490 completed unguarded calculations from the ledger of their start and end times. The numerical computations contributed 232 guarded runs, 197 of them successful, with union 47.94 h and sum 53.94 h; the independent checks contributed 641 guarded runs, 557 of them successful, and the 1,490 ledger calculations, with union 12.05 h and sum 14.16 h. The recorded computation union, 49.08 h at 08:00:00Z, therefore reached 48 h between the two cuts. Of its increase of 4.04 h over the 45.04 h at the 04:00:00Z cut, 3.57 h lie between the two cuts and 0.47 h before the 04:00:00Z cut, within the sixteenth slice of the long run, which was in progress at that cut and therefore not counted there (checked independently). Like the other figures it is a recorded interval union, not continuous useful work or active investigator time.

Recorded compute is the process wall time of recorded runs, not CPU time. It includes failed diagnostics, such as the stopped benchmark of section 7, and actual computations that run fixture checks. Synthetic fixture run records, self-test child records and captured copies are excluded, so their invented or duplicated intervals are not counted.
- At the 04:00:00Z cut the numerical computations contributed 204 resource-guarded runs, 169 of them successful. Their union is 43.85 h and their sum 48.90 h.
- At the same cut the independent checks contributed 583 resource-guarded runs, 515 of them successful, and 1,437 completed unguarded calculations from a ledger of their start and end times. Their union is 11.74 h and their sum 13.85 h.
- Unguarded runs outside that ledger are not counted, and runs still in progress at the cutoff are not counted, so these figures are lower bounds. The second slice of the repaired pilot was in progress at the 03:23:19Z cutoff, the fifth at the 05:40:21Z cutoff, the assessment run after the fifth at the 06:39:10Z cutoff, the first slice of the long run at the 07:38:33Z cutoff, the third halving assessment, on state 18, at the 08:38:49Z cutoff, the second slice of the long run at the 09:38:09Z cutoff, the third slice of the long run at the 10:38:13Z cutoff, the fourth halving assessment, on state 30, at the 11:38:51Z cutoff, the fourth slice of the long run at the 12:38:03Z cutoff, the fifth slice of the long run at the 13:38:11Z cutoff, the sixth slice of the long run at the 14:51:00Z cutoff, the seventh slice of the long run at the 16:30:00Z cutoff, the eighth slice of the long run at the 18:00:00Z cutoff, the tenth slice of the long run at the 20:00:00Z cutoff, the thirteenth slice of the long run at the 00:00:00Z cutoff, the sixteenth slice of the long run at the 04:00:00Z cut and the twentieth slice of the long run at the 08:00:00Z cut.
- Unions are formed from the individual intervals across all sources, not by adding unions.
- A classification of the union at the 04:00:00Z cut by completion only, also reproduced independently, gives 39.98 h covered by the 684 successful guarded runs or by the 1,437 ledger calculations and a further 5.06 h, in 133 separate intervals, covered only by the 103 other guarded runs; at the 08:00:00Z cut the same classification gives 44.02 h and a further 5.06 h. Failed computations may still be useful research, for example a failed benchmark that exposed a defect, and successful runs may include stalls, so neither part measures useful or useless work, and no figure of this section measures active investigator time.

Each working session is restarted by the supervisor after about six hours. The restarts up to the 04:00:00Z cut were at 06:41Z, 07:15Z, 13:16Z, 19:11Z and 21:01Z on 2026-10-24, 03:00Z, 08:58Z, 14:47Z and 20:47Z on 2026-10-25 and 02:45Z on 2026-10-26 for the coordination session, at 07:15Z, 13:16Z and 19:16Z on 2026-10-24, 01:17Z, 07:11Z, 13:08Z and 19:07Z on 2026-10-25 and 01:04Z on 2026-10-26 for the computation session and at 09:01Z, 15:00Z and 21:00Z on 2026-10-24, 02:58Z, 08:56Z, 14:55Z and 20:52Z on 2026-10-25 and 02:52Z on 2026-10-26 for the verification session, each with a gap of about 30 s. Between the two cuts the only restart was that of the computation session at 07:00Z on 2026-10-26, with a gap of about 30 s. No interval without any open session occurred up to 2026-10-26T08:00:00Z.

Optimizer time is counted only from registered optimizer run directories. It is the run-call wall time of completed slices with strictly paired start and end records, bracketed by a verified guard result and an assessment gate that was not skipped. Interrupted slices, cached states, development fixtures and copies are never counted. Each pilot directory was registered before its launch.

Slice 19 ran trials 84 to 88, of which trial 86 was rejected and trials 84, 85, 87 and 88 were accepted as states 75 to 78, and ended at its time reserve at 00:15:06Z with no assessment due, and slice 20 started at 00:15:14Z. Slice 20 ran trials 89 to 94, of which trials 89 and 92 were rejected and trials 90, 91, 93 and 94 were accepted as states 79 to 82, and ended at its time reserve at 01:27:48Z; the sixth halving assessment, the seventh full assessment counting the seed assessment, on state 82, followed from 01:27:55Z to 02:25:04Z, and slice 21 started at 02:25:05Z. Slice 21 ran trials 95 to 101, of which trials 95, 98 and 101 were rejected and trials 96, 97, 99 and 100 were accepted as states 83 to 86, and ended at its time reserve at 03:28:49Z with no assessment due, and slice 22 started at 03:28:58Z; by the data cut it had committed trials 102 to 104, of which trial 104 was rejected and trials 102 and 103 were accepted as states 87 and 88, and it was running at that time. Of the twenty-two slices of the repaired pilot and the long run up to the data cut, the fourth was interrupted by the planned SIGKILL test and is not a completed slice, the twenty-second was running at the cut, and the other twenty completed. The twenty completed slices, the interrupted fourth slice, the committed trials 102 to 104 of the twenty-second, the first eighty-eight accepted states, the sixteen rejected trials 9, 61, 65, 68, 71, 74, 77, 80, 83, 86, 89, 92, 95, 98, 101 and 104, the six halving assessments, both restart tests, the stop and the start of the long run are checked independently and reported below. At the data cut the incumbent was state 88, the last state accepted before the cut (item 7); no continuum bound on the residual maxima is reported for any state after state 82. Trials, states and results after the data cut are not reported; the run continued.

Benchmark of the long-run configuration.
- The full feasible-chart gate finished on 2026-10-24 with the result in section 4. The benchmark started on 2026-10-24 at 17:13Z with three worker counts (4, 8 and 12). The coordination session stopped that run at 18:21Z, after its first configuration. The reason: the 12-worker configuration was projected to exceed the memory limit of the resource guard on the guard's measure. That measure is the sum of per-process resident memory, which counts the pages a forked worker shares with its parent once per worker. The timings of the completed 4-worker configuration are kept as descriptive scaling data.
- The benchmark was restarted at 18:22Z with the production configuration only (8 workers, 4 cache-construction threads), under the same frozen code and limits. The resource guard stopped it at 2026-10-24T22:20:27Z, after 14,314 s, because the one-minute load average of the whole shared host reached 1.517 per logical CPU against the guard ceiling of 1.5. One of its 6,734 guard samples reached the ceiling. The guard sent SIGTERM, the run record has success false and return code -15, and the benchmark summary was never written. The strict verdict is therefore FAIL (incomplete). The guard samples establish the trigger only. They do not identify the processes that raised the load, and no cause is asserted here.
- The benchmark wrote five complete scientific records, each bound to the frozen code manifest: the record of the production configuration with all its per-operation timings, on which every check that such a record can show passes; two full assessments, with walls of at most 4,539 s and 4,107 s and the identical tight J_abs 4.268372607095638e-9; and two spot checks of at most 485 s and 360 s. Its other files are partial running telemetry and the failed guard result. The planned third assessment and spot check, and the parent-side records of the subprocesses, were never written and cannot be evaluated.
- Recorded exception. The campaign proceeded on these written records without rerunning the benchmark. A third pair could have shown variability or a failure, not only another wall sample, so omitting it is a time-budget tradeoff against the 48-hour campaign. The exception and the figures below were audited independently.
- Measured costs at the production configuration, from one record with one to five samples per operation (descriptive): a rejected trial takes about 864 s and an accepted one about 986 s including the first Jacobian assembly. About 649 s of each trial is the independent integral. The memory peak was 131,536 MiB on the guard's measure.

Launch configuration.
- The guard limits of the campaign were set from these records by rules fixed before the benchmark: a memory limit of max(163,840, ceil(1.3 x 131,536.14)) = 170,997 MiB, and 6,600 s per guarded assessment, because 1.3 x 4,539 s, about 5,901 s, is below 6,600 s. The load ceiling stayed at 1.5 per logical CPU. When a slice is stopped by that ceiling, the supervisor waits and attempts the next slice, subject to the load and the remaining budgets, so repeated load spikes can prevent progress. When an assessment fails under the guard, for any reason, the supervisor halts for diagnosis (section 9).
- The launch code manifest differs from the benchmark manifest only by two added files: the new limit file and the supervisor version that reads it. Two sessions read it back independently before the launch. Its SHA-256 is 0481a8ae91ca713f4986edfedb6267e3eae2357196695d792281900fd7777a46.
- Before the launch, a guarded replay under the benchmark manifest reloaded the seed and reproduced its stored training-mesh residual bit for bit (checked independently). The launch manifest leaves every numerical file unchanged. At its initialization the first pilot's driver replayed the seed again, with difference norm 0.0 (checked independently).

First pilot (closed).
- It started from the seed at 2026-10-24T23:11:06Z under that manifest, with slices of at most 3,300 s, at most 12 trials per slice, 8 workers and the default trust radius. A trial starts only if at least max(900 s, 1.5 x the largest trial wall) of the slice remains.
- Its first guarded run, the full assessment of the seed, ended at 00:16:33Z with guard success after 3,928 s and a memory peak of 18,832 MiB. It gave the tight J_abs 4.268372607095638e-9, and its 5,070 scientific values equal those of the benchmark assessment (checked independently). No pool-exit hang was recorded during it. The assessment code does not install the signal handler described next, and every recorded hang came later.
- Defect. Before it creates its fork-based worker pools, the campaign driver installs a SIGTERM handler that only sets a stop flag, and the forked workers inherit it. When a pool is closed, the Python library holds a queue lock while it sends one end marker per worker. A worker that loses the race for that lock blocks. The library then sends it SIGTERM, which only sets the flag, so the parent waits for that worker forever. The benchmark and assessment programs install no such handler and were not exposed.
- Interventions. Under a recorded decision of the coordinating session, only the blocked pool worker was killed (SIGKILL), and only when it was idle and its parent was waiting in the pool shutdown, after the pool had returned its results. There were six such interventions, at 00:57:17Z and 01:00:10Z by hand and at 01:02:24Z, 01:04:50Z, 01:14:46Z and 01:15:41Z by a watchdog process. Each is recorded with process stacks, and the last two also with a check that the process identity was unchanged. The record numbers skip 0006 because of the helper's counting rule; no intervention is missing. The watchdog was stopped at 01:20:16Z, after the run had ended.
- Slice 1 ran from 00:16:34Z to 01:04:50Z (2,897 s, memory peak 131,624 MiB). Its first hang occurred in the whole-axis validation of the initialization, and the first four interventions fall in this slice. It spent the whole slice in initialization and ended at the time reserve without a trial, with guard success. The driver's independent J_abs at the seed was 4.2683726070956366e-9.
- Slice 2 resumed from the committed state at 01:04:58Z. The supervisor was sent SIGTERM at 01:05:22Z so that it would start no further slice, and a stop-request file was created at 01:16:28Z. The slice finished its trial and closed at 01:19:30Z with guard success after 872 s, with a memory peak of 126,649 MiB. All pilot processes had exited by 01:19:50Z.
- The single trial was accepted. On the training mesh the objective fell from 4.26837260709569e-9 to 3.858288531911697e-9. The predicted decrease was 4.1008414e-10 and the measured one 4.1008408e-10 (ratio 0.99999983). The step norm was within 1e-12 relative of the trust radius, 4.62e-5, with KKT residual 2.1e-14 and largest Taylor constraint 5.5e-14. The new state has c_l = 1.8089923252006004 and a_0 = 14.767547808041526.
- An exact check binds the recorded step by hash to an independently retained copy of the seed's finite matrix. In that finite model the step's exact KKT ratio is 1.4e-14, it lies inside the recorded radius, and its quadratic-model suboptimality is at most 1.4e-22, or 3.4e-13 of the predicted decrease. The production value 2.1e-14 differs from the exact 1.4e-14 by 49.5 percent. At a cancellation residual of size 1e-14 this is consistent with rounding, but the production matrix was not retained, so the difference cannot be attributed conclusively and it is not established that the production matrix equals the retained one entrywise. This concerns one linearized subproblem and says nothing about stationarity of the nonlinear problem, a continuum maximum or a PDE solution.
- Its whole-axis J_abs is 3.8582885319116871340e-9, from an exact rational enclosure of width 1e-145 based on the proved finite-basis integral formula. The driver's independent value 3.8582885319116485e-9 lies within 3.9e-23 of it, far inside its stated integration error of 4.2e-19. A separate 50-digit polar quadrature agrees to a relative 1.6e-24 at step 0.05 and 5.1e-29 at step 0.025. An independent check of the seven committed events, the stored arrays and the candidate state confirmed the trial record. Its far field is in section 8.
- Certified decrease. The seed's whole-axis J_abs has an exact rational enclosure of the same kind, 4.2683726070956797e-9; the integral value 4.268372607095638e-9 of section 5 differs from it by 9.8e-15 relative. Interval subtraction of the two enclosures, with digamma values bounded rigorously, gives a decrease of 4.1008407518399257e-10, or 9.6075 percent of the seed value, and 0.99999983 of the predicted decrease. An independent quadrature agrees. This compares two approximate profiles. It does not establish a vanishing PDE residual or a PDE solution, and says nothing about stationarity, convergence, a continuum maximum or the repaired pilot.
- Certified point values. A numerical sampling of this state (26 grids, 899,150 point evaluations counted with multiplicity) found its largest E_f at (90.17465435701071, 0) and its largest E_rho at (0, 268.4935476912699), both exact binary64 points. Certified rational interval evaluation there gives E_f > 1.0960826604136e-4 and E_rho > 7.1566729121813e-5, so these are lower bounds for the continuum suprema. No tight upper bound and no location of a maximum is established. The certificate is conditional on an exact polynomial reduction of the checkpoint, which an independent evaluation from the stored coefficients reproduces to about 1e-38 relative. The seed values of section 3 (E_f = 1.2385e-4, E_rho = 7.1658e-5) come from a different sampling design (32 fixed regions and local refinement), so the two pairs are not a like-for-like comparison.
- Scope. This state is one accepted step of a closed run. It is an approximation whose density residual grows at infinity, so it is not an exact PDE solution, and its stationarity has not been established. Both slice walls and the trial wall (642 s) contain blocked intervals, so they are not throughput data, and none of the pass criteria below is evaluated on this run.

Repaired pilot.
- The repair adds one line to the campaign driver: forked children reset SIGTERM and SIGINT to the default action, so the library's SIGTERM ends a blocked idle worker as intended. The new code manifest (SHA-256 dacb203633d450ac9af62393244f88aa8aba7a8cc9416afbd1c2e84efb723037) differs from the launch manifest only in that file. Every numerical module is byte-identical. Two sessions read the installed manifest back independently before the launch.
- In a separate experiment on the pool shutdown, 38 of 250 pool exits hung with the inherited handler and none of 690 with the repair, and every completed map equalled its serial evaluation. This supports the mechanism and the repair. It is not a failure probability.
- The repaired pilot started from the seed at 2026-10-25T01:26:13Z in a new directory, with the same configuration and without a watchdog. Every pass criterion below is evaluated on this run alone.
- Seed assessment. Its first guarded run, the full assessment of the seed, ended with guard success after 3,211 s and a memory peak of 17,847 MiB. It reproduced the first pilot's J_abs, 4.268372607095638e-9, and all 5,070 scientific values with binary64 equality, and its three 90,624-entry production arrays are bitwise identical to those of the first pilot (checked independently).
- Slice 1, from 02:19:44Z to 03:09:31Z. The driver initialized from the seed, replaying its stored training-mesh residual with difference norm 0. It then ran four trials, all accepted, and ended normally at the time reserve with guard success after 2,990 s and a memory peak of 133,540 MiB on the guard's measure. About 605 s of the worker wall preceded the first trial; the four iteration walls were 514 to 664 s, median 602 s. No guard stop occurred and no swap was used. 112 of the 1,429 resource samples show elevated block paging or writeback while free memory stayed ample, so the slice was not free of resource warnings. All of this was checked independently against the eight committed events, the completion record and the resource samples.
- Slice 2, from 03:09:41Z to 03:43:50Z. The driver resumed from the fourth accepted state after replaying its stored residual with difference norm 0 against the tolerance 1.0e-18; recovery and replay took 186 s. It then ran two trials, both accepted, with iteration walls of 925 and 936 s, and ended normally at the time reserve with guard success after 2,049 s and a memory peak of 132,796 MiB on the guard's measure. No guard stop occurred and no swap was used. 90 of the 962 resource samples show elevated block paging or writeback while free memory stayed ample. Both trials used the same 1,216,512 independent integration nodes as trials 1, 2 and 4 (trial 3 used 559,104), so their longer walls are not explained by a larger recorded node count; their cause is not established. All of this was checked independently against the committed events, the completion record and the resource samples.
- Replay of the first accepted state. A separate guarded replay reloaded the first accepted state with the frozen model and reproduced its stored training-mesh residual with zero differing binary64 entries (difference norm 0 against the tolerance 1.25e-18). This was checked independently from the saved arrays. The freshly computed residual was not serialized, so the check binds the recorded production computation to the saved arrays and is not a second byte-level comparison. With the replay at initialization this gives zero-step replay equality at the seed and at the first accepted state.
- Accepted states, checked independently. Each J_abs is an exact rational enclosure of width 1e-145 based on the proved finite-basis integral formula, and a separate 50-digit polar quadrature reproduces it at step 0.05 to a relative 5e-22 or better for states 1 to 7 and, for states 8 to 88, to between 4.9e-22 and 4.0e-19 (largest for state 54); halving the step reduced the reported discrepancy in every state, for states 8 to 88 to between 1.4e-28 and 4.5e-28. Each decrease is the interval difference of two enclosures, with width 2e-145. The driver's own independent integral lies within 4e-23 of the enclosure in every state. Its stated integration error is between 4.2e-19 and 4.3e-19 for states 1, 2 and 4 to 9, between 4.315e-19 and 4.745e-19 for states 10 to 13, 15 to 35 and 37 to 83, 9.3e-16 for state 3, 2.444e-16 for state 14, 2.491e-16 for state 36, 1.240e-15 for state 84, 1.079e-15 for state 85, 7.806e-16 for state 86, 6.416e-16 for state 87 and 3.857e-16 for state 88, where its adaptive radial rule stopped one order earlier and reported the difference of its last two radial orders; for states 14 and 36 the exact enclosures show the stored values to be within 3.3e-24 and 9.6e-26 of J_abs, and for states 84 to 88 within 1.8e-25. The integration uncertainty of a trial, the sum of the stated errors of its starting and its proposed state, is at most 5.6e-3 of the predicted decrease in every accepted trial (largest for trial 99, from state 84 to state 85; it exceeds 3.2e-5 only in trials 97, 99, 100, 102 and 103, which end at states 84 to 88; otherwise the two largest values occur at trials 37 and 38, which end and start at state 36, with the maximum at trial 38, and the others are at most 4.3e-6, largest for trial 16, which starts from state 14), against the limit of 5 percent. The first state has the coefficients and c_l of the accepted state of the closed first pilot. The KKT residual is the recorded relative residual of the trust-region subproblem of the linearized model.

  J_abs values are the enclosures rounded to 17 digits; the other columns are roundings of the recorded or exact values. Each step had a recorded norm within 1e-12 relative of its trust radius. The radius doubled after each of the first seven acceptances and after states 9, 59, 60 and 62 to 88, was held after states 8, 10 to 58 and 61, whose measured decrease was between 0.25 and 0.75 of the predicted one, and was quartered after the rejected trials 9, 61, 65, 68, 71, 74, 77, 80, 83, 86, 89, 92, 95, 98, 101 and 104 (below); state 9 was proposed from state 8, state 60 from state 59, state 63 from state 62, state 65 from state 64, state 67 from state 66, state 69 from state 68, state 71 from state 70, state 73 from state 72, state 75 from state 74, state 77 from state 76, state 79 from state 78, state 81 from state 80, state 83 from state 82, state 85 from state 84 and state 87 from state 86. The trust radius column gives the radius of the step that produced the state. From the seed to state 5 the certified decrease is 2.2784974802472382e-9, or 53.380941 percent, so state 5 is the first state below half the seed value. The first halving assessment therefore became due at the end of the slice; the slice accepted state 6 before it ended, and the assessment ran on the incumbent state 6 at the end of the slice (Halving assessment, below). Each decrease from the seed is the interval difference of two enclosures; at the six assessed states 6, 10, 18, 30, 48 and 82 it is 2.7895534212646210e-9, or 65.354028 percent, 3.6218396425532332e-9, or 84.852940 percent, 3.9487214820660681e-9, or 92.511171 percent, 4.1322847027243807e-9, or 96.811715 percent, 4.2060167156382535e-9, or 98.539118 percent and 4.2382432441290011e-9, or 99.294125 percent; at state 88, the last state of the table, it is 4.2409315461370695e-9, or 99.357107 percent. Here and below, "the value assessed at state N" is the validated J_abs that the assessment ledger records for state N, the value the cadence check compares with (section 9). State 9 is the first state below half of the value assessed at state 6, so the second halving assessment became due at the end of slice 5; the slice accepted state 10 before it ended, and that assessment ran on state 10 (Second halving assessment, below). State 18 is the first state below half of the value assessed at state 10, about 3.2327e-10, so the third halving assessment became due at the end of slice 7, the slice in which state 18 was accepted, and it ran on state 18 (Third halving assessment, below). States 19 to 27 lie above half of the value assessed at state 18, about 1.5983e-10, so no full assessment was due at the end of slice 8, which ended at state 24. State 28 is the first state below that half, so the fourth halving assessment became due at the end of slice 9, the slice in which state 28 was accepted; the slice accepted states 29 and 30 before it ended, and that assessment ran on state 30 (section 9). States 31 to 45 lie above half of the value assessed at state 30, about 6.8044e-11, and no full assessment was due at the end of slice 10, which ended at state 36, and of slice 11, which ended at state 42. State 46 is the first state below that half, so the fifth halving assessment, the sixth full assessment counting the seed assessment, became due at the end of slice 12, the slice in which state 46 was accepted; the slice accepted states 47 and 48 before it ended, and that assessment ran on state 48 (section 9). States 49 to 79 lie above half of the value assessed at state 48, about 3.1178e-11, and no full assessment was due at the end of slice 13, which ended at state 53, of slice 14, which ended at state 59, of slice 15, which ended at state 63, of slice 16, which ended at state 66, of slice 17, which ended at state 70, of slice 18, which ended at state 74, and of slice 19, which ended at state 78. State 80 is the first state below that half, so the sixth halving assessment, the seventh full assessment counting the seed assessment, became due at the end of slice 20, the slice in which state 80 was accepted; the slice accepted states 81 and 82 before it ended, and that assessment ran on state 82 (section 9). Among accepted states 1 to 88, state 44 has the lowest ratio of measured to predicted decrease, 0.52762; from state 27 the ratio fell at every step to state 42, rose at state 43, fell at state 44, rose at every step to state 60, fell at state 61, rose at every step to state 63, fell at state 64, rose at state 65, fell at state 66, rose at state 67, fell at state 68, rose at state 69, fell at state 70, rose at state 71, fell at state 72, rose at state 73, fell at state 74, rose at state 75, fell at state 76, rose at state 77, fell at state 78, rose at state 79, fell at state 80, rose at state 81, fell at state 82, rose at state 83, fell at state 84, rose at state 85, fell at state 86, rose at state 87 and fell at state 88, and it lies below the 0.54884 of state 8 at states 38 to 46. The center residual is exactly zero in every state, because the center coefficient is eliminated analytically.
- Certified point value of state 6. A numerical sampling of state 6 (26 grids, 899,150 point evaluations counted with multiplicity) found both its largest E_f and its largest E_rho at the exact binary64 point (0, 270.0080795244217) on the y axis, where E_f = E_rho because R_rho = y R_f. Certified rational interval evaluation there gives E_f = E_rho > 7.08691152072989e-5, a strict lower bound for both continuum suprema. This is one point and two quotients. No location of a maximum is established; a continuum upper bound follows below. The certificate is conditional on an exact polynomial reduction of the state, which an independent evaluation from the stored coefficients reproduces at this point to about 5e-40 relative.
- Matched sampled comparison. The seed was resampled on the same two fixed grids, with the same four-level refinement protocol and the same number of evaluations, although its adaptive points differ. The largest sampled E_f fell from 1.2386e-4 at the seed, at (8.683015161429145, 0), to 7.0869e-5 at state 6, by 42.78 percent; the largest sampled E_rho fell from 7.1659e-5, at (0, 268.08573713924113), to 7.0869e-5, by 1.10 percent. These are reductions of sampled maxima. By themselves they do not establish that either continuum supremum decreased or that any sampled point is a maximizer; the next item establishes both decreases by other means.
- Continuum upper bounds for state 6 and comparison with the seed (checked independently). The residual of state 6 is exactly a finite sum of terms q^(ell delta) P(1 - q) cos(n theta), with q = 1/(1+r^2), delta = (1 - 1/c_l)/2, ell = 1 to 8, even n up to 38 and polynomials P with 6,325 rational coefficients in total. Rounding these coefficients to multiples of 2^-192, covering 2^-40 <= q <= 1 by 170 rational intervals subdivided in x = cos(2 theta) into 646 closed rectangles, each bounded through an exact polynomial in both variables, and bounding the remaining range q < 2^-40 exactly (below 8.915e-7 after normalization) gives, at every r >= 0 and every angle, E_f <= 0.000071473092108459168332170946660088897 and E_rho <= 0.00007131234559204440929241542272528. Both are below 143/2000000. Every rectangle bound and the remaining-range bound were recomputed independently by a different algebraic route, with a third method for the radial power enclosures, and agreed to a relative 5.3e-50. At 832 sampled interior points of the rectangles, an independent evaluation from the stored coefficients agreed with the exact reduction and stayed below the rectangle bounds. For the seed, certified rational interval evaluation at the two exact binary64 points of the matched comparison gives E_f(8.683015161429145, 0) > 0.000123863020978337 and E_rho(0, 268.08573713924113) > 0.0000716586070397144, by two independent routes, each reproduced by two further evaluations. A point value is a lower bound for a supremum. Hence both continuum suprema of state 6 are strictly smaller than those of the seed, for E_f by more than 42.296 percent and for E_rho by more than 0.483 percent. These are lower bounds on the relative decreases, not their values. The seed here is the pilot's initial state; its free coordinates equal those of the immutable seed exactly. The comparison concerns the two saved potentials only. It determines no supremum value or location and does not cover the intermediate states. Both potentials have nonzero Taylor defects and a growing unnormalized density residual (section 8), and nothing follows about stationarity, convergence or a PDE solution.
- Brackets for state 6 (checked independently). The point value above and these upper bounds give 7.0869e-5 < sup E_f < 7.1474e-5 and 7.0869e-5 < sup E_rho < 7.1313e-5 for state 6; the upper bounds exceed the point value by less than 0.86 and 0.63 percent. No location of either supremum is established.
- Continuum upper bounds for state 8 and comparison with state 6 and the seed (checked independently). The same method, applied to the exact residual of state 8 (6,325 rational coefficients, 167 radial intervals and 326 rectangles, and the remaining range q < 2^-40 bounded below 7.986e-7 after normalization), gives at every r >= 0 and every angle E_f <= 0.000069991489307921408793323396919114768 and E_rho <= 0.00006972388200131611510481304021077, both below 7/100000. Every rectangle bound and the remaining-range bound were recomputed independently. With the certified point values of state 6 and of the seed above, both continuum suprema of state 8 are strictly smaller than those of state 6, for E_f by more than 1.238 percent and for E_rho by more than 1.615 percent, and than those of the seed, by more than 43.492 and 2.699 percent. These are lower bounds on the relative decreases, not their values. No point value, supremum value or location is established for state 8, the comparison does not cover the intermediate states and shows no monotonicity along the sequence, and nothing follows about stationarity, convergence or a PDE solution.
- Halving assessment (checked independently). The full assessment of state 6 ran under the guard from 03:43:56Z to 04:50:30Z, 3,994 s, with guard success, a memory peak of 18,873 MiB on the guard's measure, no guard stop and no swap, and it requested no scientific stop. Its tight J_abs, 1.4788191858310475e-9, lies within 1.12e-23 of the exact enclosure of state 6, inside its reported error of 4.2e-22. Its center and Taylor parameters, far-field moments and E_1, E_2, E_3, its 71 relative monitors and its 2,565 large-radius samples were reconstructed independently; the largest relative discrepancy among the monitors is 3.5e-14. Its sampled extrema were checked at the reported points only, and the maximality of its grid searches was not re-established. Like the other sampled maxima, these are not continuum bounds, and the large-radius samples corroborate the growing residual of section 8 without proving an asymptotic statement.
- Slice 3 and the SIGTERM restart test (checked independently). Slice 3 resumed from state 6 after the halving assessment. At 04:53:31Z SIGTERM was sent to the campaign worker alone, with no stop-request file; the guard was not signalled. The signal arrived in the model phase of trial 7, before any trial of the slice had been committed, and not between trials as planned; this placement was accepted as a recorded deviation. The driver finished trial 7, which gave state 7, and closed the slice at that trial boundary, 714.5 s after the signal, with completion status stopped_by_request and guard success (return code 0, no stop reasons, 896 s, memory peak 126,627 MiB, no guard stop and no swap). The driver's slice-end label 'operator stop request' names its internal stop flag and does not mean that a stop-request file existed. The supervisor took its branch for a stop without a stop-request file, which counts as a process fault (counter 1, inferred from the source because this branch prints no counter), and waited 60 s. After the cadence check (2.2 s, no assessment due), slice 4 resumed from state 7 at 05:06:28Z. The stored training-mesh residual was replayed with difference norm 0 against the tolerance 6.72e-19 (a norm comparison, not a separate byte comparison), with seven committed proposals, no orphaned files and an unchanged trajectory of 16 rows. The old guard and worker processes were gone. So a SIGTERM takes effect at the next trial boundary, not at once.
- Continuum upper bounds for state 10 and comparison with state 6 and the seed (checked independently). The same method, applied to the exact residual of state 10 (6,325 rational coefficients, 167 radial intervals and 321 rectangles, and the remaining range q < 2^-40 bounded below 7.602e-7 after normalization), gives at every r >= 0 and every angle E_f <= 0.000064634477867426734082100307886312160 and E_rho <= 0.00006429861185922468300578186325692, both below 13/200000. Every rectangle bound and the remaining-range bound were recomputed independently. With the certified point values of state 6 and of the seed, both continuum suprema of state 10 are strictly smaller than those of state 6, for E_f by more than 8.797 percent and for E_rho by more than 9.271 percent, and than those of the seed, by more than 47.817 and 10.270 percent. These are lower bounds on the relative decreases, not their values. No comparison with state 8 follows, since state 8 has upper bounds only. No point value, supremum value or location is established for state 10, the comparison does not cover the intermediate states and shows no monotonicity along the sequence, and nothing follows about stationarity, convergence or a PDE solution.
- Continuum upper bounds for state 13 and comparison with state 6 and the seed (checked independently). These are lower bounds on the relative decreases, not their values. No comparison with states 8 and 10 follows, since they have upper bounds only. No point value, supremum value or location is established for state 13, the comparison does not cover the intermediate states and shows no monotonicity along the sequence, and nothing follows about stationarity, convergence or a PDE solution.
- Continuum upper bounds for state 15 and comparison with state 6 and the seed (checked independently). The same method, applied to the exact residual of state 15 (6,325 rational coefficients, 170 radial intervals and 463 rectangles, and the remaining range q < 2^-40 bounded below 6.682e-7 after normalization), gives at every r >= 0 and every angle E_f <= 0.000045395026705827740287740505733097416 and E_rho <= 0.00004539502670582774028774050573310, both below 1/22000. Together with the upper bounds this brackets both suprema of state 16: 2.512045095710577e-5 < sup E_f < 4.32675e-5 and 2.512045095710577e-5 < sup E_rho < 4.30366e-5. - Continuum upper bounds and fixed-point values for state 18, and comparison with state 6 and the seed (checked independently). The same method, applied to the exact residual of state 18 (6,325 rational coefficients, 172 radial intervals and 471 rectangles, and the remaining range q < 2^-40 bounded below 6.221e-7 after normalization), gives at every r >= 0 and every angle E_f <= 0.000039993565829963762551909645249474731 and E_rho <= 0.00003996680943854751879491510589456, both below 1/25000. These are lower bounds on the relative decreases, not their values. Exact interval evaluations at the same two fixed points give 1.008285990391049e-5 < E_f(64, 0) < 1.008285990391050e-5 with E_rho(64, 0) = 0, and 2.552036545156749e-5 < E_f(0, 64) = E_rho(0, 64) < 2.552036545156750e-5, reproduced independently. Together with the upper bounds this brackets both suprema of state 18: 2.552036545156749e-5 < sup E_f < 3.99936e-5 and 2.552036545156749e-5 < sup E_rho < 3.99669e-5. - Slice 4 and the SIGKILL restart test (checked independently). Slice 4 resumed from state 7 at 05:06:28Z after a replay with difference norm 0 and a recovery of 157 s, and ran trial 8, which was committed at 05:21:08.575Z as a normal accepted trial (state 8, iteration wall 722 s). The trial-9 intent was written at 05:22:31.618Z. At 05:22:37.106Z the campaign worker alone was stopped with SIGSTOP in the evaluation phase of trial 9, before any trial-9 commit; its process group then held no pool process, so the step that kills pool children had nothing to kill. SIGKILL followed about 0.9 s later, and the worker was gone at 05:22:40.990Z. The guard result is the expected failure: success false, return code -9, no stop reasons, 971.1 s, memory peak 132,799 MiB on the guard's measure, no guard stop and no swap in any of its 463 samples, 108 of which show the same paging or writeback warnings as before. The supervisor counted process fault 2, waited 120 s, ran the cadence check (2.2 s, no assessment due) and resumed slice 5 from state 8 at 05:24:42Z. Its first records mark slice 4 as interrupted: no orphaned proposal (trial 9 had an intent only), eight committed proposals, an unchanged trajectory of 18 rows, and a replay of the stored training-mesh residual with difference norm 0 against the tolerance 6.06e-19; recovery took 139 s. The repeated trial 9 has an intent whose fields other than its time stamp equal those of the interrupted one. The driver recorded 936 s for the interruption; that interval runs from the start of trial 8 to the startup of the new worker and contains the committed trial 8. The wall interval from the trial-8 commit to the kill is 89.4 s; it is not a measurement of lost CPU time or useful work. Not exercised by this test: cleanup of live pool children, recovery of an orphaned proposal and the loss of the guard or the supervisor.
- Slice 5 (checked independently). Slice 5 ran from 05:24:42Z to 05:59:44Z and ended normally at the time reserve, with guard success after 2,102 s and a memory peak of 132,761 MiB on the guard's measure. It ran trials 9, 10 and 11, with iteration walls of 709, 575 and 679 s: trial 9 was rejected, trials 10 and 11 were accepted (states 9 and 10). Trial 10 started from the same incumbent and reused the model that trial 9 had built, so its recorded model time repeats that of trial 9 and its iteration wall equals its trial wall; the model time built new in this slice is 165 s, not the sum 249 s of the recorded model times.
- Rejected trial 9 (checked independently). Proposed from state 8 with the radius 5.91e-3, the step norm was within 1e-12 relative of the radius, with KKT residual 6.8e-13. The proposed state has c_l = 1.8085653703582314, a_0 = 14.231672042076133 and largest Taylor defect 1.1e-13. Its exact whole-axis J_abs, enclosed in an exact rational interval of width 1e-145 near 9.1905194748218020e-10, is above that of state 8 by 8.9192e-12, or 0.97999 percent, so the ratio of the measured to the predicted decrease is negative, about -0.028070. Under the trust-region rule the trial was rejected, state 8 stayed the incumbent and the radius was quartered to 1.48e-3. The proposed state is a rejected candidate, not an iterate; its density residual also grows at infinity (section 8). The trial matrix and its KKT residual were not reproduced independently.
- Second halving assessment (checked independently). The full assessment of state 10 ran under the guard between slices 5 and 6, 3,366 s, with guard success, a memory peak of 18,403 MiB on the guard's measure, no guard stop and no swap at the sampled times. Its tight J_abs, 6.465329645424409e-10, lies within 5.54e-24 of the exact enclosure of state 10, inside its reported error of 3.8e-22. Its center and Taylor parameters, far-field moments and E_1, E_2, E_3, its 71 relative monitors and its 2,565 large-radius samples were reconstructed independently; the largest relative discrepancy among the monitors is 5.3e-14. As for the first, its sampled extrema were checked at the reported points only, and they are not continuum bounds.
- Model check at accepted state 10 (checked independently). The complete finite model at state 10, all 90,624 mesh rows and 855 feasible columns, was assembled again with the same field evaluator but a separate tangent composition and factorization; every recomputed residual entry equals the saved one bitwise. With the fixed column scales of the optimizer, the trust-region subproblem of trial 12, the trial taken from state 10, was solved again by a direct SVD, by an SVD of a rescaled QR factor, both keeping every positive computed singular direction, and by a regularized Cholesky route refined against the full matrix. The predicted decrease, 8.026992659816098e-11, agrees with the recorded prediction of trial 12 to less than 5.8e-13 relative, and the steps agree with the recorded step to within 4.0e-12 of the trust radius. Evaluated in this matrix, the recorded step has a KKT residual of 6.5e-13 to 6.6e-13 of the gradient norm, depending on the accumulation (recorded 6.5e-13). This checks the finite linearized model and the proposed step of one trial. The raw field derivatives come from the same evaluator, which was checked against 170-digit references at six points only, so a systematic evaluator error elsewhere would be shared. The check does not bound the error of the field derivative, does not certify a rank (351 of the 855 computed singular values lie above the diagnostic floor 1.9e-10, and the smallest, about 1e-16, are not resolved) and does not show stationarity. Trial 12 was accepted on its nonlinear evaluation, which this check does not touch.
- Slice 6 and the end of the pilot (checked independently). Slice 6 started at 06:55:56Z from state 10 and ran trials 12 and 13, both accepted (states 11 and 12), with iteration walls of 735 and 652 s. The long run had been released by then (below). While the slice ran, with its live worker confirmed, a stop-request file was created. The driver checks for it only between trials, so trial 13 finished and was committed normally; the slice then closed with completion status stopped_by_request and guard success (return code 0, no stop reasons, 1,572 s, memory peak 132,793 MiB on the guard's measure, no guard stop and no swap in its 750 resource samples, 134 of which show paging or writeback warnings). The supervisor took its branch for a stop-request file and exited at 07:22:09Z without an end assessment. A process read after that found the old supervisor, guard, worker and pool processes gone, and no fault, diagnosis, refusal or budget marker present; this is a point-in-time observation.
- Scope. These states are accepted optimizer iterates. They are approximations whose density residual still grows at infinity (section 8), so they are not exact PDE solutions. Their stationarity has not been established; section 10 gives exact derivatives of states 6, 8, 10, 16, 60 and 88 on sets with fixed Taylor defects, two at an exactly Taylor-feasible reference of state 18 and at the minimum of J_abs on one exact line through it, and one at an exactly Taylor-feasible reference of state 88, which do not change this. The certified decreases of J_abs alone compare approximate profiles and say nothing about stationarity, convergence or a continuum maximum. Certified point values of E_f or E_rho are reported here for states 1, 6, 16, 18, 22, 30, 44, 48, 82 and 88 only, as in item 7; none are reported for any other accepted state.
- Pass criteria, read from the committed events only:
  - at least 10 trials, of which at least 3 are accepted;
  - zero-step replay equality at the seed and at the first accepted state;
  - KKT residual at most 1e-8 for every trial, and for every accepted state a converged Taylor lift with raw constraint maximum below 1e-10;
  - logged predicted and measured decreases, and the logged changes of c_l, a_0 and the width observables W_x and W_y;
  - independent integration uncertainty at most 5% of the predicted decrease;
  - the code manifest digest on every event;
  - restart tests with SIGTERM between trials and SIGKILL inside a trial;
  - resource projections, an independent re-evaluation of at least one accepted state, and the time ledger.
- Two independently written readers of the committed events must both pass, but that alone does not release the long run. Every item above needs its own evidence, including the separately recorded signal and process evidence of the restart tests, the guarded replays, the re-evaluation of an accepted state, the resource projection and the time ledger. The long run then needs an explicit release with independent concurrence. It continues in the same directory with 5,000 s slices and no per-slice trial cap.
- Status of these items at the release, from the independently checked records of the first 26 committed events: eleven trials with ten accepted; zero-step replay equality at the seed and at the first accepted state; recorded KKT residuals at most 1.1e-11 and Taylor defects at most 4.8e-13 in every trial; in the accepted states, stated integration errors at most 2.1e-6 of the predicted decrease, against the limit of 5 percent; the SIGTERM and SIGKILL restart tests, with the untested branches stated above; independent re-evaluations of accepted states 1 to 10. Apart from the step of trial 1, checked on a retained copy of the seed matrix, and the step of trial 12 (Model check, above), the trial KKT residuals have not been recomputed from full-mesh matrices.
- Release (checked independently). On the first 26 committed events, two independently written readers of the committed events passed and agreed on every decision, and an independent reader of the stored arrays and a gate readback passed as well. Every item above was confirmed in its recorded scope, the replay of the first accepted state by the separately guarded replay described above, and the restart tests with their three untested branches. The peak summed memory of the completed guarded runs was 133,540 MiB against the limit of 170,997 MiB, and the projected disk use for 72 hours with a twofold margin, 1.6 GB, was below the budget of 40 GB, with 141.8 GiB free. The time ledger was audited independently at 06:39:10Z. The independent concurrence was recorded at 2026-10-25T07:08:26Z and the release at 07:13:26Z. The release does not change any statement about stationarity, convergence or PDE solutions.
- Long run (checked independently through its first replay). After the verified stop of slice 6 the stop-request file was kept, unchanged, under a timestamped name. At 07:24:21Z the same supervisor code was started again as a new detached process on the same directory, guard session, code manifest, seed, coordinate map and trust radius, resuming from state 12 with 5,000 s slices, 8 workers, no trial cap (the default per-slice maximum of 10^9 trials) and no radius override, with the code tree of the run made read-only. The cadence check found no assessment due (2.1 s). Slice 7, the first slice of the long run, began with a replay of the stored training-mesh residual of state 12 with difference norm 0, no orphaned proposal and no interrupted slice. The states keep their numbering; nothing was relabelled or reset. The first trial of the long run, trial 14, was accepted as state 13 (event 31; table above), with an iteration wall of 660 s and the radius held. Trial 15 was accepted as state 14 (event 32), with an iteration wall of 498 s and the radius held; its validation stopped at the second radial order, as in trial 3, which explains the shorter wall and the larger stated error above. Trial 16 was accepted as state 15 (event 33), with an iteration wall of 737 s and the radius held, and trial 17 as state 16 (event 34), with an iteration wall of 696 s and the radius held. Trial 18 was accepted as state 17 (event 35), with an iteration wall of 679 s, and trial 19 as state 18 (event 36), with an iteration wall of 715 s, both with the radius held. At this radius the measured-to-predicted ratio fell at every trial from trial 12 to trial 19, from 0.6951 to 0.6031, and stayed above 0.25, below which the radius would shrink. Slice 7 ended normally at its time reserve at 08:33:13Z (event 37, completion status checkpointed_slice), with guard success after 4,131 s, of which the worker spent 4,129 s, 143 s of them on recovery, and 17,653 s of CPU time in the trial loop. - Rejected trial 61 (checked independently). The first trial of slice 15 and the first at the doubled radius, it was proposed from state 59 with the radius 5.91e-3; the step norm was within 1e-12 relative of the radius, with KKT residual 8.2e-12. The proposed state has c_l = 1.8075126461687914, a_0 = 10.545299052573306 and largest Taylor defect 5.6e-14. Its exact whole-axis J_abs, enclosed in an exact rational interval of width 1e-145 near 4.7013491901740309e-11, is above that of state 59 by 2.4046e-12, or 5.3903 percent, so the ratio of the measured to the predicted decrease is negative, about -1.0025. Under the trust-region rule the trial was rejected, state 59 stayed the incumbent and the radius was quartered to 1.48e-3. The next trial, trial 62, was proposed from state 59 at that radius and accepted as state 60. - Rounding indicator at state 30 (checked independently). For each sampled training node, the run's rounding indicator compares the unweighted residual recovered from the stored weighted residual with the allowance 2^-53 (S + |v|), where S is the node's sum of absolute terms and v the recovered value. Exact certificates show that at state 30 this unweighted indicator is exceeded at five training nodes, by up to 9.25 percent, and a numerical census of all 90,624 training nodes at 180 digits found no other node above it. An independent census of all nodes, with a different evaluator at 50 digits, finds the same five nodes above the unweighted indicator and no node above the weighted one, with the same largest ratios. Wherever its 50-digit values were compared with the 180-digit census, they agree to within 2.4e-10 relative, the worst cases being nodes of the smallest radii. At six selected training nodes (76, 89, 100, 385, 562 and 84011), both ratios recomputed at 100 and 140 digits agree with the 180-digit census at all 25 stored digits. A further, independently written census of all 90,624 training nodes at 80 digits agrees with the 180-digit census node by node, to a relative 5e-25 in both ratios as stored to 25 digits: the weighted ratio stays below 1 at every node, with largest value 0.49323 at the same node, and the unweighted ratio exceeds 1 at the same five nodes, with largest value 1.09248. This is an agreement of two numerical evaluations, not an exact certificate for every node. None of the five is among the 45 nodes evaluated by the fourth assessment (34 drawn at random over 7 radial bands for 32 requested, and 11 distinct targeted nodes), whose largest ratios were 0.317 (random) and 0.423 (targeted). At the five nodes the stored weighted residuals equal the correctly rounded products of the stored root weights and the correctly rounded residuals, and the weighted inequalities used in the objective's error estimate hold; in the numerical census the largest weighted ratio over all nodes is below 0.4933. The excess comes from recovering the unweighted value by a final binary64 division: the exact quotient of the stored weighted residual by the stored root weight passes the indicator at all five nodes (ratios 0.669 to 0.785), and the final rounding of that quotient makes it fail. If the value before weighting was the correctly rounded residual, the recovered value carries three roundings of the same sign, each within the allowance, against an allowance designed for one; the residual actually computed by the run before weighting was not saved, so this attribution is conditional. Separately, the stored root-weight error figure h, which enters only the allowance of the weighted inequalities, through the coefficient 3 x 2^-53 + h of each absolute weighted residual, is the nearest binary64 value of a 40-digit evaluation and lies 1.0e-32 below the exact maximum, so it is not an outward bound; its effect on that coefficient is a relative 2e-17. The root weights themselves are within 1.77 x 2^-53 of their exact values at every node, inside the run's 8 x 2^-53 check. The indicator is a sampled diagnostic, not a bound at every node, and the earlier assessment passes are statements about their sampled nodes. The run's rule, constants and code were not changed; a stop raised by a later assessment at its own nodes would stand. Two independent integer-interval implementations, at 256 and 384 bits, prove the weighted inequalities at all 90,624 training nodes of state 30, with the largest ratio below 0.49323, and both find exactly the same five nodes above the unweighted indicator (checked independently). On the same saved nodes and weights, the exact finite sum that the training-mesh objective evaluates, Q = sum_i w_i R_f(x_i, y_i)^2 / (pi A^2) with the run's reference scale A = 23.95065822465609, exceeds the exact J_abs of state 30 by 5.223e-25, a relative 3.838e-15 (checked independently). This holds for this state and this mesh only and is not a general quadrature error bound.

## 8. Growth of the unnormalized density residual

Status: the baseline part below is verified. For optimizer states, the accepted state of the closed first pilot, the first eighty-eight accepted states of the repaired pilot and its long run, exactly Taylor-feasible references of states 18 and 67 and the rejected trials 9, 61, 65, 68, 71, 74, 77, 80, 83, 86, 89, 92, 95, 98, 101 and 104 are verified; states accepted after the data cut 2026-10-26T04:00:00Z are not reported.

The objective measures R_f, and R_rho = y R_f is the residual of the original density equation. As r tends to infinity, the behaviour of R_rho is decided by finite exact arithmetic on the coefficients. For r > 1, each generator has the convergent expansion

    r^(2k) (1 + r^2)^(p_j - k) = sum_(q >= 0) binom(p_j - k, q) r^(3 + gamma - j lambda - 2q),   gamma = 1/c_l, lambda = 1 - gamma.

Substituting it into rho, psi and the transport term gives, uniformly in theta,

    R_rho(r, theta) = sum_(h=0..7) sum_(q=0..Q) r^(gamma - h lambda - 2q) E_(h,q)(theta) + O(r^(gamma - 2(Q+1))).

Here each E_(h,q) is a sine polynomial. Its coefficients are rational when c_l, the stored binary64 coordinates and the fixed normalization constants are read as the rationals they denote, and the center coefficient is eliminated in exact arithmetic. A copy of the coefficients rounded to any finite precision is not used: rounding the coefficients one by one can change an exact tail coefficient by a large factor (in a constructed example, one changes by a factor of about 36). The sector (h, q) = (0, 0) vanishes identically, because c_l gamma = 1 and no transport pair contributes to h = 0. Terms with exactly equal exponents are added in exact arithmetic. Then the largest exponent whose group is nonzero and lies above gamma - 2(Q+1) is the true leading power of R_rho in the L2(0, pi/2) angular norm. The sign of that power decides growth. When E_1 = E_(1,0) is nonzero, the leading power is 2 gamma - 1. This means growth for c_l < 2, a bounded but nondecaying residual for c_l = 2, and decay for c_l > 2. Decay with c_l < 2 is possible only if E_1 vanishes exactly. The derivation was checked twice: once as a written proof with an independent exact rational implementation, and once as a separate step-by-step derivation.

Baseline (the 860 seed is the baseline written in the 860 basis), at c_l = 1.80899...:
- The leading exponent is 2 gamma - 1 = 860221935092683/8146977319648309, approximately 0.105587864228, which is positive. The density residual therefore grows like r^0.1056.
- R_f = R_rho / y decays like r^(-0.894). This is consistent with a finite J_abs while R_rho grows.
- Norms from the exact rational Fourier coefficients of the seed. Each squared norm is pi times a rational number; the values shown are the square roots rounded to binary64: ||E_1|| = 0.4403534214951272, ||E_2|| = 6.363132453703603, ||E_3|| = 31.72147169432992.
- Leading coefficient: R_rho behaves like r^(2 gamma - 1) E_1(theta). The corresponding coefficient of R_f = R_rho / y is E_1(theta) / sin(theta), whose angular L2 norm is 0.5184741022646407.
- Unresolved angular modes: E_1 is a sine polynomial in the odd harmonics up to 39, while the density family represents odd harmonics only up to 19. The part of E_1 in harmonics 21 to 39 has norm 0.14464% of ||E_1|| (about 6.369e-4). For E_2 and E_3 the parts above harmonic 19 are 0.0289% and 0.0125% of their norms.
- Frozen production monitor at the seed (verified). The far-field monitor of the frozen campaign code, run on the seed inside a complete development assessment with the same exact rational input, reports the same leading exponent 860221935092683/8146977319648309, marked as determined (the leading nonzero exponent exceeds the omitted bound, and every retained group above that leading exponent is exactly zero), the same ||E_1||, ||E_2||, ||E_3|| and quotient norm in all printed digits, and the same parts above harmonic 19 (norms 6.369e-4, 1.838e-3 and 3.966e-3). An independent exact reconstruction of that assessment reproduced these numbers.
- Exactly Taylor-feasible reference of section 4 (verified, exact). The leading exponent is the same, 860221935092683/8146977319648309. ||E_1|| > 0.44 is proved by an exact rational comparison (with pi > 3.14). Each of E_1, E_2 and E_3 differs from the seed's in L2 norm by less than 2^-47. Imposing the center relation and the five Taylor conditions exactly therefore leaves the growth of the density residual at infinity in place.
- Exact descent states of section 4 (verified, exact). This covers the rational line state t = 1/16384 and the rational second-curve state s = 2^-19. For both, the leading exponent is the same, 860221935092683/8146977319648309, and ||E_1|| > 0.44 by exact rational comparison. The norms ||E_1||, ||E_2||, ||E_3|| decrease slightly from the reference: to 0.4403532, 6.3631316 and 31.721470 for the line state, and to 0.4403525, 6.3631315 and 31.721469 for the curve state. The L2 norms of the changes in the functions E_N are below 2^-13. The part of E_1 above harmonic 19 stays at 0.14464% of its norm. The density residual still grows at infinity in these states.
- These are statements about exact values at the stated coefficients. They are not a bound at any finite radius.

In the repaired pilot and the long run, through event 155 of the run record, the optimization code minimized the absolute objective J_abs and nothing else, and its potential family kept the fixed angular indices m <= 9 (860 generators; 855 free directions when the five Taylor conditions have full rank). The code places no constraint, penalty or acceptance threshold on E_1, E_2 or E_3 or on their norms: acceptance of a feasible trial step depends only on the decrease of J_abs, the gain ratio, an integration-accuracy test and a rounding test. E_1, E_2 and E_3, with their represented parts (harmonics up to 19) and the omitted remainder, are computed in exact rational arithmetic from the stored coefficients at the scheduled assessments and recorded; they are monitored, not optimized. Checks can still stop the run: failed assessment checks (non-finite or malformed output, failure of the exact Poisson identity, an incomplete record, the origin-truncation monitor or the reference-value spot checks) and a missing assessment when one is due; the optimizer's numerical stops (integration accuracy, trust-subproblem accuracy, repeated trust-region rejection, rank loss or an exceptional parameter value, the precision floor, a small projected-residual indicator, and a change of the frozen code); resource-guard limits, which halt or pause the run; and repeated process faults. Trial points that violate the Taylor conditions, the coefficient bounds, the nonzero-a_0 condition or finiteness are rejected and the trust radius is reduced, and the center elimination, the parity restriction and the canonical Poisson representation are built into the parametrization. All seven assessments recorded through event 155 were complete and raised no stop. The recorded source for the earlier, unrepaired pilot differs by one line added in the repaired version, which resets the termination-signal handlers in forked worker processes; that pilot's runtime behavior was not checked.

Optimizer states.
- Accepted state of the closed first pilot (section 7), at c_l = 1.8089923252006004. The leading exponent is 2 gamma - 1 = 860222093051481/8146977161689511, approximately 0.1055878857, and it is determined and positive, so the density residual still grows like r^0.1056. The norms are ||E_1|| = 0.4402256805273892, ||E_2|| = 6.363089090257716 and ||E_3|| = 31.72220542231634. Against the seed, ||E_1|| and ||E_2|| decreased slightly and ||E_3|| increased slightly. The parts above harmonic 19 are 0.1657%, 0.0372% and 0.0191% of the norms. These values come from an exact rational reconstruction, and a separate 60-digit computation from the stored coefficients reproduced them to at least 40 digits.
- Exactly Taylor-feasible reference of that state (verified, exact). Changing nine branch-zero coefficients (k = 3 or 4) of the accepted state, each by less than 3.91e-17, gives a rational potential with the same c_l in which the center relation and the five Taylor conditions hold exactly; in the stored binary64 state the largest of the five Taylor defects is 5.45e-14. Its local feasible dimension is 855. The leading exponent is unchanged, 860222093051481/8146977161689511, ||E_1|| > 0.44 by an exact rational comparison (with pi > 3.14), and E_1, E_2 and E_3 change in L2 norm by less than 2^-48, 2^-48 and 2^-47. The coefficient of sin(39 theta) in E_1 depends only on the untouched highest moment and is exactly unchanged and nonzero. Exact Taylor feasibility therefore again leaves the growth of the density residual in place. This reference is not a campaign checkpoint. An interval computation from the exact coefficients gives its J_abs minus that of the accepted state as Delta_J = 1.8614488811329369e-23, which is positive, so its J_abs is about 3.85828853191170575e-9, still below the seed value. A separate 50-digit quadrature corroborates Delta_J to a relative 4.2e-28; it is not an error bound. No stationarity or PDE solution is claimed for this reference.
- Accepted states 1 to 88 of the repaired pilot and its long run (section 7). State 1 has the coefficients and c_l of the accepted state above. For states 2 to 88 the values come from an exact rational reconstruction at the stored coefficients and the exact binary64 c_l, and a separate 60-digit computation from the stored coefficients reproduces them to at least 17 significant digits, the precision of the compared values for state 2, to a relative 6e-23 or better for states 3 to 7, to a relative 3e-39 or better for states 8 to 16, to less than 3.4e-39 for E_1 and less than 2e-39 for E_2 and E_3 for state 17, to 1.1e-39 or better for state 18, to less than 2e-39 for states 19, 20 and 23 to 26, for states 21 and 22 to less than 3.8e-39 and 4.2e-39 for E_1 and less than 7e-40 for E_2 and E_3, for states 27 to 30 to less than 4.3e-39, 4.8e-39, 5.8e-39 and 3.4e-39 for E_1 and less than 7e-40 for E_2 and E_3, for states 31 and 32 to less than 1.8e-38 and 9.3e-39 for E_1 and less than 3e-41 and 5e-40 for E_2 and E_3, for state 33 to less than 4e-40 for all three, for state 34 to less than 5.5e-39 for E_1 and less than 6e-40 for E_2 and E_3, and for states 35 to 82 to less than 9.4e-38 for E_1 and less than 5e-39 for E_2 and E_3. For states 83 to 88 this comparison and the exact comparison below are not checked independently. For states 8 to 17 and 19 to 30 an exact evaluation from both coefficient sets shows that these differences come from rounding the stored coefficient strings to 50 significant digits (exactly less than 3e-39 relative, except less than 3.4e-39, 3.8e-39, 4.2e-39, 4.3e-39, 4.8e-39, 5.8e-39 and 3.4e-39 for E_1 of states 17, 21, 22, 27, 28, 29 and 30). The same exact comparison shows this for states 31 to 34, with exact relative differences less than 1.8e-38, 9.3e-39, 4e-40 and 5.5e-39 for E_1 and less than 3e-41, 5e-40, 4e-40 and 6e-40 for E_2 and E_3 (checked independently). For states 35 to 82 it gives exact relative differences less than 9.4e-38 for E_1 and less than 5e-39 for E_2 and E_3 (checked independently). In every state E_1 is a nonzero sine polynomial, so the leading exponent is 2 gamma - 1, determined and positive, and the density residual grows like r^(2 gamma - 1), with the exponents of the table (0.10558 to 0.10665).

  Along these states ||E_1|| falls at every accepted state; it lies 1.1 percent below its seed value at state 6, 13.6 percent at state 10, 30.7 percent at state 18, 48.3 percent at state 30 and 72.5 percent at state 88. ||E_2|| first falls slightly, at states 1 and 2, and then rises up to state 6; ||E_3|| rises at every state up to state 6. Both are largest at state 6, about 0.065 and 0.094 percent above their seed values, and fall at every later accepted state; from state 7 on both lie below their seed values, at state 18 by 19.2 and 9.4 percent, at state 30 by 34.1 and 19.6 percent and at state 88 by 59.3 and 41.7 percent. The parts of E_2 and E_3 above harmonic 19 grow from 0.029 and 0.013 percent at the seed to 0.276 and 0.288 percent at state 7, 0.398 and 0.413 percent at state 10, 0.429 and 0.449 percent at state 11, 0.458 and 0.482 percent at state 12, 0.486 and 0.512 percent at state 13, 0.513 and 0.539 percent at state 14, 0.541 and 0.565 percent at state 15, 0.568 and 0.590 percent at state 16, 0.597 and 0.616 percent at state 17, 0.626 and 0.643 percent at state 18, and further at every later state up to state 34, to 1.042 and 0.994 percent at state 30 and 1.141 and 1.037 percent at state 34; from state 24 on, the part of E_2 exceeds that of E_3. As fractions of the norms, the part of E_2 rises up to state 42, to 1.197 percent, and falls at every later state, to 0.850 percent at state 88, and the part of E_3 rises up to state 35, to 1.037 percent, falls at every state from state 36 to state 58, to 0.805 percent, rises at every state from state 59 to state 74, to 0.820 percent, and falls at every state from state 75 to state 88, to 0.800 percent. The part of E_1 above harmonic 19, which comes from the branch-0 part of E_1 alone and cannot be produced by the branch-1 moments (section 10), rises as a fraction of its norm at every state from the seed to state 61, from 0.145 to 3.510 percent, and falls at every later state, to 3.329 percent at state 88; in absolute L2 norm it is 6.369e-4 at the seed and 6.17e-3 at state 34, is largest among states 30 to 88 at state 35 (6.18e-3) and falls at every later state, to 4.03e-3 at state 88, while J_abs falls by more than 99.35 percent from the seed. From state 5 to state 50 c_l fell at every accepted step, so the exponent 2/c_l - 1 rose, to 0.10664 at state 50; from state 51 to state 88 c_l rose at every accepted step, so the exponent fell, to 0.10587 at state 88. These two trends are measured side by side; they do not show which harmonics account for the decrease of J_abs. These are exact statements at the stated coefficients, not bounds at any finite radius.

Changing nine branch-zero coefficients (k = 3 or 4) of the exact reading of the checkpoint, each by less than 1.369e-17 (the Taylor jets by less than 1.103e-14), gives a separate rational potential with the same c_l and a_0 in which the center relation and the five Taylor conditions hold exactly; in the stored binary64 state the largest of the five Taylor defects is 3.81e-14. E_1, E_2 and E_3 change in L2 norm by less than 2^-49, 2^-49 and 2^-48, and all three norms decrease. An exact computation gives its J_abs minus that of the checkpoint strictly between -9.169194798895017e-23 and -9.169194798895016e-23, a relative change of -2.87e-13; a separate quadrature corroborates this difference and is not an error bound. ## 9. Long-running process and ETA
End of the run (recorded when this report was closed). The independent verifier observed the run at 2026-10-26T13:20:00Z: the supervisor 1270876, with unchanged start ticks, leader of its own session with no controlling terminal, the guard 3388239 and the campaign worker 3388245 with eight pool children, all at nice 19 on the twelve assigned logical CPUs, no stop-request, diagnosis, fault, driver-refusal or exhausted-budget marker present, and slice 30 running. The coordinating session read the same three processes at 13:23:08Z. At the end of the run, between 2026-10-26T13:28:17Z and 13:29:02Z, the shutdown of the campaign sent SIGTERM and then SIGKILL to every process whose working directory lay in the campaign's directories, which included the supervisor, the guard and the worker. When the process table was read at 13:30:12Z none of the three existed, no marker file had been written, the last heartbeat was dated 13:28:11Z (156 trials, 131 accepted) and the guard record of slice 30 had no result. These end-of-run observations were made by the coordinating session and were not checked independently; the trial counts after the data cut are taken from the run record only. So no long run is left active. The last committed state, accepted state 131 of trial 156 (commit event 224 at 13:23:53Z), and every earlier commit remain in the run directory (described under Output records below); trial 157, in progress at the termination, is lost, as the design intends for work in progress at a kill. A restart with the start line of the run book is possible in principle, but this termination of the supervisor, the guard and the worker together was not tested: the unfinished guard record must first be diagnosed and the guard session and committed state validated and released, as described under Restart for an unclean shutdown. The ETA figures below describe the run at the data cut; for a resumed run they give the time to spend the rest of the configured budget, less the guarded time used after the cut, which was not checked independently.

Over the 1,821 guard samples of slice 21, the last complete slice, the one-minute load of the shared host was approximately 33.4 to 69.6, memory PSI full avg10 was at most 4.25 and no swap was used; the solver used 14,331.7 s of CPU in 3,825.5 s of guard wall, 3.7 cores on average. The run directory's file system is shared with other users, and its free space changed in both directions for reasons that were not established; read at 2026-10-26T04:00:05Z, 89.6 GiB of 875.0 GiB were free. The guard stops the run when less than 10 GiB is free, and nothing restarts it automatically. ETA at the data cut, stated as arithmetic on measured rates and not as a forecast: over the complete slices 7 to 21 the optimizer made 88 trials, 5.335 per hour of slice guard time (59,379.367 s) and 4.388 per hour of all guarded time of the long run, including 4 full assessments and 12 cadence checks (72,195.034 s); with at most 223.56 h of budget left, the rest of the budget corresponds to about 981 to 1,193 further trials. The budget cannot be used up before 2026-11-04T11:33:05Z, the time reached if guarded runs follow each other without gaps from the start of the slice then in progress, about 9.3 days after the cut. This is not a statement that the run will make these trials or continue to that date; a stop rule, a guard stop or a halt can end it earlier, and the time to convergence cannot be guaranteed.
