Its far-field monitor gives L2 norms 0.12112557210824139, 2.5869753237096993 and 18.48072103892784 for E_1, E_2 and E_3 and a determined leading density-residual exponent 0.10586538035904068, which is exactly 2/c_l - 1 for the stored c_l = 1.8085383949271123 and is positive, so the record classifies the leading density residual as growing with radius. The largest sampled values of E_f and E_rho are 1.2196532464105754e-5 on the fixed grids, at a point near (0, 74.30), and 1.2196685033154225e-5 after local refinement, at (0, 74.49521655431515); these are values at stored sample points, not bounds, suprema or maximizers. An independent readback of the assessment record, completed at 2026-10-26T05:02:26Z, confirmed these values within its finite numerical scope, evaluating the residual independently at the recorded maximizing locations and other recorded points; it did not rerun the complete grids or searches and did not establish a continuum maximum or maximizing location of state 88, numerical reliability outside the checked configurations, unconditional sampling guarantees or continuous resource bounds. The standalone assessment ran in its own guard session; it is not charged to the run's guarded budget and is not an entry of the run's assessment ledger, so it does not change the next halving threshold. Read at 2026-10-26T05:03Z, after the standalone assessment had finished, the longest iteration observed in the run was still 936.1060573919676 s (trial 6), so the reserve was unchanged at 1404.1590860879514 s.

- Process. The campaign supervisor, a file of the frozen code manifest, was started at 2026-10-25T01:26:13Z with setsid and nohup, at nice 19, on twelve assigned logical CPUs. It is process 70811 on the campaign host and leads its own process group and session. No terminal is attached, and its standard input, output and error are redirected to the null device. The process table showed the supervisor, the guard and the seed-assessment worker at 2026-10-25T01:46:14Z, and the same supervisor with the guard and worker of the halving assessment at 2026-10-25T03:54:33Z. The first pilot's supervisor was process 3601953, started at 2026-10-24T23:11:06Z. For the long run the same supervisor file was started again at 2026-10-25T07:24:21Z with setsid and nohup, at nice 19, on the same twelve logical CPUs, with the code tree of the run read-only and the same directory and guard session: process 1270876, leader of its own process group and session, with guard 1271208 and campaign worker 1271246, the worker in its own process group with one BLAS thread (checked independently). The pilot's supervisor 70811 and all its descendants were gone before that start. A process read at 08:23:05Z showed the three processes running, with the supervisor's standard input, output and error on the null device. A process read at 2026-10-25T09:45:09Z showed the same supervisor 1270876 with the guard 2794715 and campaign worker 2794717 of slice 8, one at 2026-10-25T11:19:16Z the same supervisor with the guard 3286111 and campaign worker 3286118 of slice 9, one at 2026-10-25T12:07:38Z the same supervisor with the guard 3767128 and the assessment processes 3767136 and 3767145 of the fourth halving assessment and one at 2026-10-25T12:41:13Z the same supervisor with the guard 4179878 and campaign worker 4179879 of slice 10. Independent process reads at 2026-10-25T14:58:39Z and 16:30:09Z each found eleven processes in the supervisor's family: the same supervisor 1270876, the guard and campaign worker of the running slice (950625 and 950647 of slice 12 at the first read, 2497607 and 2497665 of slice 13 at the second) and eight pool workers of that campaign worker, each at nice 19 and restricted to the same twelve logical CPUs, with no observed writable file descriptor into the protected tree and no observed mapping of a file of the source repository. An independent process read from 2026-10-25T18:00:00.61Z to 18:00:00.62Z found three processes in the family: the same supervisor 1270876 and the guard 3773929 and campaign worker 3773930 of slice 14, each at nice 19 and restricted to the same twelve logical CPUs, with no observed file descriptor or mapping that refers to the source repository; no pool worker was present in that read, and nothing follows from it about pool processes at other times. An independent process read from 2026-10-25T20:00:00.038Z to 20:00:00.071Z found eleven processes in the family: the same supervisor 1270876, the guard 1391427 and campaign worker 1391454 of slice 16 and eight further processes, each at nice 19 and restricted to the same twelve logical CPUs, with no observed file descriptor or mapping that refers to the source repository. An independent process read from 2026-10-26T04:00:00.047Z to 04:00:01.042Z found eleven processes in the family: the same supervisor 1270876, the guard 110543 and campaign worker 110559 of slice 22 and eight child processes of that campaign worker, each at nice 19 and restricted to the same twelve logical CPUs, with no observed mapping of a file of the protected tree and no observed writable file descriptor into it. Each read is a sequence of per-process reads, not an atomic snapshot, and covers only its own time. The supervisor runs every slice and every assessment under the resource guard. Its branches:
  - A slice stopped by the guard only for reasons in a fixed list (the host-wide load, memory-pressure and pageout ceilings, and the per-run wall-time budget): the supervisor waits with backoff, records an acknowledgement and returns to the start of its loop (the guarded assessment check, then the next slice), subject to the load and the remaining budgets. If the acknowledgement is refused because the conditions still fail, the next guarded assessment check can be refused as well, and the supervisor then halts for diagnosis. Further progress is not guaranteed.
  - A slice stopped by the guard for any other reason: fault marker, halt.
  - A process fault of a slice (no guard result, a guard failure without a stop reason, a nonzero return code other than the driver refusal, or a missing or unbound completion record) or an unexpected completion status: the fault count rises by one and the supervisor retries with backoff. When the count reaches five without an intervening checkpointed slice, it writes the fault marker and halts.
  - A slice ended by SIGTERM while no stop-request file exists: the supervisor counts this as a process fault and retries with backoff, but this branch has no five-fault test. Repeated terminations of this kind therefore do not halt the supervisor by themselves; the raised count takes effect only at the next fault of the kind above.
  - The guarded assessment check at the start of each loop, which runs a full assessment when one is due (at the seed, when the validated J_abs of the incumbent has fallen to half of the value that the assessment ledger records for the last assessed state, and after every 100 accepted steps): if its guard run fails for any reason, including a host-wide load stop, the supervisor writes the diagnosis marker and halts. The same marker is written when an assessment requests a scientific stop and when a slice ends in a scientific stop state. The end assessment after a late stop request follows a different path, described under Safe stop.
  - A driver refusal: driver-refusal marker, halt. A slice whose guard result reports the exhausted cumulative session budget: exhausted-budget marker, halt. If the budget is exhausted during the guarded assessment check at the start of a loop, that guard failure takes the assessment branch above and writes the diagnosis marker instead. During the end assessment after a late stop request it is only logged, and the supervisor exits.
  - Loss of the campaign worker alone. If the campaign worker dies while its pool workers survive, for example after an external SIGKILL, the guard records the stop reason 'worker leader exited with surviving process-group members', ends the process group and sets a hold. That reason is not in the recovery list, so the supervisor writes the fault marker and halts. This is a safe halt that needs a diagnosis, not an automatic restart. It follows from the code and has not been exercised. The SIGKILL restart test of the pilot uses a controlled sequence instead (the worker is stopped, its pool workers are killed, then the worker), so even a clean recovery in that test does not establish recovery from this case. Work in progress at a kill is lost by design; what survives is the last committed checkpoint and the journal records.
  - The process does not survive a host reboot. Recovery after a reboot has not been tested (see Restart).
- Output records. The campaign outputs hold the supervisor process identifier and log, the guard session, the committed events, the accepted snapshots and the assessments. The first pilot is kept separately as a closed record. The accompanying artifacts identify the records and provide operational commands. The working copy and these outputs lie outside the protected tree.
- ETA model. This is computed from the benchmark records before any trial, so it is a model and not a measurement.
  - Inputs: a slice startup of about 334 s (cold cache build, Jacobian assembly, SVD and chart), a rejected trial of about 864 s, an accepted trial of about 986 s, 60 s of process overhead per slice, and the slice reserve rule of section 7.
  - Initialization. The startup figure contains no validation. A new run performs one whole-axis validation before its first commit, which took a median of 649 s in the benchmark records; resumed slices do not repeat it. With that added, and every other assumption kept, the first 3,300 s slice holds one trial instead of two at each assumed acceptance rate. The later rates below are unchanged. The first pilot's slice and trial walls contain blocked intervals and are not used.
  - Result: with assumed acceptance rates of 0.25 to 0.75 this gives two trials per 3,300 s pilot slice (3.1 to 3.3 trials per hour) and four trials per 5,000 s long-run slice (3.4 to 3.6 trials per hour). One hundred trials therefore take about 28 to 32 hours, plus about 75 minutes per full assessment.
  - Example: under the model the repaired pilot needs six slices for its ten trials (one in the first slice and two in each later one), about 5.6 h after its seed assessment if no load stop occurs. This is a model figure, not a forecast of the live run. The live run recorded six trials in its first two slices, all checked independently.
- Measured throughput.
  - The first slice of the repaired pilot (checked independently) spent about 605 s before its first trial, including the whole-axis validation, and then ran four trials with iteration walls of 514 to 664 s, median 602 s. It held four trials where the model expected one, so the model is conservative for that slice. The second slice (checked independently) spent 186 s on recovery and replay and then ran two trials with iteration walls of 925 and 936 s; the cause of the longer walls is not established (section 7). The third and fourth slices ran one trial each (740 and 722 s), because they ended by the planned SIGTERM and SIGKILL restart tests. The fifth slice (checked independently) spent 139 s on recovery and replay and ran three trials with iteration walls of 709, 575 and 679 s before it reached its reserve after 2,102 s. Its second trial reused the model of the rejected first one (section 7), so its iteration wall contains no new model build; the model time recorded for that trial was not spent again and is not counted twice. The sixth slice ran two trials (735 and 652 s) before the normal stop request of the pilot. The seventh, the first slice of the long run, ran six trials, trials 14 to 19, with iteration walls of 660, 498, 737, 696, 679 and 715 s, median 688 s, and ended at its time reserve after 4,131 s of guarded time, 5.23 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently). It held six trials, as in the 600 s scenario below, but ran 4,131 s rather than about 3,786 s because its walls were longer. The eighth slice, the second of the long run, spent 133 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 20 to 25, with iteration walls of 613 to 641 s, median 614 s, and ended at its time reserve after 3,848 s of guarded time, 5.61 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently), close to the 600 s scenario below. The ninth slice, the third of the long run, spent 134 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 26 to 31, with iteration walls of 613 to 616 s, median 615 s, and ended at its time reserve after 3,822 s of guarded time, 5.65 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently), also close to the 600 s scenario below. The tenth slice, the fourth of the long run, spent 134 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 32 to 37, with iteration walls of 614 to 615 s, except 489 s for trial 37, whose independent validation stopped early at its accuracy test, median 614 s, and ended at its time reserve after 3,697 s of guarded time, 5.84 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently). The eleventh slice, the fifth of the long run, spent 134 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 38 to 43, with iteration walls of 613 to 615 s, median 614 s, and ended at its time reserve after 3,818 s of guarded time, 5.66 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently). The twelfth slice, the sixth of the long run, spent 133 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 44 to 49, with iteration walls of 610 to 613 s, median 612 s, and ended at its time reserve after 3,805 s of guarded time, 5.68 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently). The thirteenth slice, the seventh of the long run, spent 171 s on recovery and replay and then ran five trials, trials 50 to 54, with iteration walls of 741 to 839 s, median 795 s, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 74 against 23 in the twelfth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 4,142 s of guarded time, 4.35 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); no cause of the longer walls is established. The fourteenth slice, the eighth of the long run, spent 153 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 55 to 60, with iteration walls of 631 to 687 s, median 655 s, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 56 against 74 in the thirteenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 4,091 s of guarded time, 5.28 trials per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); no cause of the shorter walls is established. The fifteenth slice, the ninth of the long run, spent 140 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 61 to 66, of which trials 61 and 65 were rejected, with iteration walls of 538 to 676 s, median 633 s, the shortest being that of trial 62, which reused the model of the rejected trial 61, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 48 against 56 in the fourteenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 3,912 s of guarded time, 5.52 trials and 3.68 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); apart from the model reuse of trials 62 and 66, no cause of the differences in the walls is established. The sixteenth slice, the tenth of the long run, spent 189 s on recovery and replay and then ran five trials, trials 67 to 71, of which trials 68 and 71 were rejected, with iteration walls of 636 to 830 s, median 737 s, trial 69 reusing the model of the rejected trial 68, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 63 against 48 in the fifteenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 3,788 s of guarded time, 4.75 trials and 2.85 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); no cause of the longer walls is established. The seventeenth slice, the eleventh of the long run, spent 159 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 72 to 77, of which trials 74 and 77 were rejected, with iteration walls of 583 to 755 s, median 707 s, trial 75 reusing the model of the rejected trial 74, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 64 against 63 in the sixteenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 4,269 s of guarded time, 5.06 trials and 3.37 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); no cause of the shorter walls is established. The eighteenth slice, the twelfth of the long run, spent 144 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 78 to 83, of which trials 80 and 83 were rejected, with iteration walls of 581 to 727 s, median 681 s, trial 81 reusing the model of the rejected trial 80, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 54 against 64 in the seventeenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 4,180 s of guarded time, 5.17 trials and 3.45 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); no cause of the shorter walls is established. The nineteenth slice, the thirteenth of the long run, spent 196 s on recovery and replay and then ran five trials, trials 84 to 88, of which trial 86 was rejected, with iteration walls of 561 to 793 s, median 719 s, trial 87 reusing the model of the rejected trial 86, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 54 against 54 in the eighteenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 3,695 s of guarded time, 4.87 trials and 3.90 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently); no cause of the longer walls is established. The twentieth slice, the fourteenth of the long run, spent 136 s on recovery and replay and then ran six trials, trials 89 to 94, of which trials 89 and 92 were rejected, with iteration walls of 580 to 870 s, median 687 s, trials 90 and 93 reusing the models of the rejected trials 89 and 92, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 59 against 54 in the nineteenth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 4,356 s of guarded time, 4.96 trials and 3.31 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently, through trial 94); no cause of the differences in the walls is established. The twenty-first slice, the fifteenth of the long run, spent 164 s on recovery and replay and then ran seven trials, trials 95 to 101, of which trials 95, 98 and 101 were rejected, with iteration walls of 433 to 577 s, median 527 s, trials 96 and 99 reusing the models of the rejected trials 95 and 98, while the sampled 1-minute load of the shared host had median 53 against 59 in the twentieth slice, and ended at its time reserve after 3,826 s of guarded time, 6.59 trials and 3.76 acceptances per hour of guarded slice time (checked independently, through trial 101); no cause of the differences in the walls is established.
  - The reserve is 1.5 times the longest iteration wall of the whole run, not of the current slice, so one slow trial lowers the trial count of every later slice. The trial walls depend on the load of the shared host, which the campaign does not control.
  - Scenarios for the long run, from the frozen start rule. After the second slice the reserve is 1.5 x 936.1060573919676 s = 1404.1590860879514 s (about 1,404 s), and it was unchanged through the twenty-first slice. With a recovery of 186 s, as in the second slice, and a constant iteration wall of 600 s, a 5,000 s slice holds six trials and ends after about 3,786 s, about 5.7 trials per hour; at 940 s it holds four trials and ends after about 3,946 s, about 3.6 trials per hour. One hundred trials then take about 17.5 to 27.4 hours, plus the full assessments. At a constant 433.4 s, the shortest measured wall through trial 101 (trial 99), a slice would hold eight trials; walls above the stored running maximum of 936.1060573919676 s raise the reserve further. These are scenarios, not a forecast.
  - Full assessments: the seed assessment took 3,211 s. One is due at the end of a slice when the validated J_abs of the incumbent is at most half of the validated J_abs that the assessment ledger records for the last assessed state, and after every 100 accepted steps; the ledger value is the J_abs measured when that state was accepted, which differs from the tight integral of its assessment by less than 1e-24. The first halving came after five accepted steps, and its assessment, on state 6, took 3,994 s (checked independently). The second came after four more accepted steps: state 9 was the first state below half of the state-6 value, the cadence check after the fifth slice found the assessment due, and it ran on state 10, the incumbent at that time, and took 3,366 s (checked independently). The third came after eight more accepted steps: state 18 was the first state below half of the state-10 value, about 3.2327e-10, and its assessment, between slices 7 and 8, took 3,328 s (checked independently). The fourth came after ten more accepted steps: state 28 was the first state below half of the state-18 value, about 1.5983e-10, the cadence check after the ninth slice found the assessment due, and it ran on state 30, the incumbent at that time, from 11:36:51Z and took 2,834 s (checked independently). The fifth came after sixteen more accepted steps: state 46, accepted in trial 47 during slice 12, was the first state below half of the state-30 value, about 6.8044e-11 (states 31 to 45 lie above it), the cadence check after the twelfth slice found the assessment due, and it ran on state 48, the incumbent at that time, from 15:33:06Z and took 3,198 s, with guard success (checked independently). The sixth came after thirty-two more accepted steps: state 80, accepted in trial 91 during slice 20, was the first state below half of the state-48 value, about 3.1178e-11 (states 49 to 79 lie above it), the cadence check after the twentieth slice found the assessment due, and it ran on state 82, the incumbent at that time, from 01:27:55Z and took 3,429 s, with guard success (checked independently). The next is due when the validated J_abs of the incumbent at the end of a slice is at most 1.5064681483339156e-11, half of the ledger value 3.012936296667831e-11 of state 82 (checked independently). How often later ones come depends on the unknown rate of decrease, so their total time is not modelled.
  - Configured budget: the guard session allows 900,000 s (250 h) of cumulative guarded run time. Under the model this is roughly 800 to 900 trials, depending on the acceptance rate and the number of full assessments. When the budget is used up during a slice, the supervisor halts with the exhausted-budget marker; when it is used up during a loop-start assessment check, it halts with the diagnosis marker (branches above). Either way the 250 h cap applies. Continuing then needs a new guard session with an explicit new budget, recorded as an event. A stop rule of the optimizer can end the run earlier. Waiting time outside guarded runs, such as the backoff after a load stop, makes the wall-clock time longer than the guarded time.
  - Caveats: the acceptance rate is hypothetical until the pilot measures it, and repeated load stops would lower these rates. A guard stop for low free memory, low free disk space (floor 10 GiB on a shared file system that had 170 GiB free at launch) or temperature is not in the recovery list, so a shortage caused by other users of the shared host would halt the run until it is diagnosed and restarted.
  - These are times to spend a trial budget. The time to convergence is unknown and cannot be guaranteed, and no convergence estimate is made.
- Monitor: use the commands in the accompanying artifacts to inspect the supervisor process, heartbeat (at most 10 minutes old while a slice runs), recent log and trajectory entries, committed snapshots and disk use. A diagnosis, driver-refusal, fault or exhausted-budget marker means the supervisor has halted and needs a diagnosis.
- Safe stop: create the stop-request marker using the command in the accompanying artifacts while a slice is running (confirm this from the live worker process).
  - A running slice finishes its current trial and commits its end record, and the supervisor exits without an end assessment. If stop request lands between slices, the supervisor first runs an end assessment.
  - The stop is complete only when all of these hold: the guard result of the stopped slice reports success and its completion record is committed and bound to that run; a process table read by PID and by parent PID shows that the old supervisor and all its guard and worker descendants are gone; and, after a late stop request, the end assessment has separately passed its guard result and a full-row read. The supervisor exits with code 0 even after a failed end assessment, so neither its exit code nor a vanished PID establishes the stop.
  - Emergency stop: send SIGTERM to the supervisor, then to the guard. Never send SIGKILL to the worker first.
- Restart:
  - Normal case only (a verified stop as above and the required release): rename the stop-request marker to a timestamped record, then relaunch the same root and guard session with the start line of the run book in the accompanying artifacts. The driver resumes from the last committed state and first replays the last accepted residual under a strict 64-eps rule.
  - Abnormal halts: the marker files above are kept, not renamed away. Each refuses the restart until its halt has been diagnosed under its own rules and released.
  - After a host reboot: untested. A restart is conditional on validating and releasing the recovered guard session and the committed state, and may need a diagnosis of the guard state left by the unclean shutdown.

## 10. Stationarity, exact solutions and the next step
No. Neither a stationary optimizer nor an exact PDE profile is supported by the evidence. No accepted state of the 860 run has been shown to be stationary; accepted states 6, 8, 10, 16 and 60, the handoff state 88 and the exactly Taylor-feasible references of states 18 and 88 are shown below not to be stationary on the stated sets. In each of the first eighty-eight accepted states the unnormalized density residual still grows at infinity (item 8), and no coefficient vector of the 860 family is an exact profile with nonzero central density slope (the theorem below).

Verified facts about the baseline accepted278:
- Its five saved Taylor (C5) defects are all exactly nonzero for the serialized binary parameters (exact rational arithmetic; maximum 4.888e-14). It is therefore not an exact C5 point. Here, as in the objective values above, the eliminated center coefficient is recomputed analytically from the other binary parameters, as the solver does. Using the rounded center value stored in the serialized file instead defines a different potential, whose whole-axis objective diverges.
- An exact C5-feasible point exists within distance 1e-12 of it. This was shown by a rational contraction certificate at fixed c_l, varying five selected coefficients: Newton correction norm 2.93e-17, contraction factor 1.83e-9. This concerns the finite-dimensional constraint, not the PDE.
- The baseline is not a stationary point of the constrained problem, even after moving to the exact C5 root. The exact C5-feasible point near the saved one (radius 1e-16, unique by the contraction certificate) has an admissible direction, the old Global coordinate 0, along which J_abs strictly decreases. The directional derivative of the objective there differs from its value at the saved point (g(0) > 5.842455572985905e-6) by at most 5.12e-14, which is shown by exact contraction inequalities together with whole-axis norm bounds for the 26 relevant generators. This is a statement about the finite family. It does not concern an optimizer run.
- A small numerically evaluated J_abs (4.2684e-9) coexists with a growing unnormalized density residual at infinity (section 3, far field). A small objective value is therefore not evidence of an exact profile.
- Accepted state 6 of the repaired pilot (section 7; verified, exact). Along one high coordinate of the optimizer's own variables, which leaves a_0, the center relation and the five saved Taylor defects unchanged, the whole-axis J_abs of state 6 has a derivative that lies, by an exact rational enclosure, strictly between 4.98534727704351e-6 and 4.98534727704352e-6, so it is positive. So state 6, taken as an exact finite potential, is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on the set that holds those defects fixed; this says nothing about an exactly Taylor-feasible point, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes, or a PDE solution. A step of -2^-23 along this direction lowers J_abs by 5.44069282231559e-13 (0.0368 percent); that comparison state was not submitted to the optimizer. The direction is the generator r^10 (1 + r^2)^(p_0 - 5) of branch 0 and mode 0.
- Accepted state 8 of the repaired pilot (section 7; verified, exact). Along the same coordinate, the generator r^10 (1 + r^2)^(p_0 - 5) of branch 0 and mode 0, which again leaves a_0, the center relation and the five saved Taylor defects unchanged, the derivative of the whole-axis J_abs of state 8 lies, by an exact rational enclosure, strictly between -2.98580940743154e-6 and -2.98580940743153e-6, so it is negative. A step of +2^-24 along this direction lowers J_abs by at least 1.65710589748187e-13 (about 0.018 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (9.0996704672106006e-10, 9.0996704672106007e-10). The scope is the same as for state 6: state 8 is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on the set that holds its saved defects fixed, and nothing follows for an exactly Taylor-feasible point, the training-mesh objective or a PDE solution. The comparison state is not an accepted iterate and was not submitted to the optimizer.
- Accepted state 10 of the repaired pilot (section 7; verified, exact). Along the same coordinate, which again leaves a_0, the center relation and the five saved Taylor defects unchanged, the derivative of the whole-axis J_abs of state 10 lies, by an exact rational enclosure, strictly between -1.352827338158872e-5 and -1.352827338158871e-5, so it is negative, as at state 8. A step of +2^-22 along this direction lowers J_abs by more than 3.032351225564876e-12 (about 0.469 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (6.4350061331688158e-10, 6.4350061331688159e-10). The scope is the same as for states 6 and 8: state 10 is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on the set that holds its saved defects fixed, and nothing follows for an exactly Taylor-feasible point, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes, or a PDE solution. The comparison state is not an accepted iterate and was not submitted to the optimizer.
- Accepted state 16 of the long run (section 7; verified, exact). Along the same coordinate, which again leaves a_0, the center relation and the five saved Taylor defects unchanged, the derivative of the whole-axis J_abs of state 16 lies, by an exact rational enclosure, strictly between -1.142832879839006e-5 and -1.142832879839005e-5, so it is negative, as at states 8 and 10. A step of +2^-22 along this direction lowers J_abs by more than 2.543289492974282e-12 (about 0.674 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (3.7502525641612347e-10, 3.7502525641612348e-10). The scope is the same as for states 6, 8 and 10: state 16 is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on the set that holds its saved defects fixed, and nothing follows for an exactly Taylor-feasible point, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes, or a PDE solution. The comparison state is not an accepted iterate and was not submitted to the optimizer.
- Accepted state 60 of the long run (section 7; verified, exact). Along the same coordinate, which again leaves a_0, the center relation and the five saved Taylor defects unchanged, the derivative of the whole-axis J_abs of state 60 lies, by an exact rational enclosure, strictly between -1.545174824455170e-7 and -1.545174824455169e-7, so it is negative, as at states 8, 10 and 16. A step of +2^-28 along this direction lowers J_abs by more than 5.447101031212128e-16 (more than 0.001245 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (4.3738249858715446e-11, 4.3738249858715447e-11). The step is smaller than the 2^-22 used at state 16 because the same rule, the largest power of two not above |q1|/(2(|q2|+|q3|+|q4|)), is applied to a derivative about 74 times smaller. The scope is the same as for states 6, 8, 10 and 16: state 60 is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on the set that holds its saved defects fixed, and nothing follows for an exactly Taylor-feasible point, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes, or a PDE solution. The comparison state is not an accepted iterate and was not submitted to the optimizer.
- The handoff state 88 of the long run (section 7; verified, exact; computed after the data cut on the state fixed at the cut). Along the same coordinate, which again leaves a_0, the center relation and the five saved Taylor defects unchanged, the derivative of the whole-axis J_abs of state 88 lies, by an exact rational enclosure, strictly between -5.339490242435027e-7 and -5.339490242435026e-7, so it is negative, as at states 8, 10, 16 and 60. A step of +2^-26 along this direction lowers J_abs by more than 7.477424932861313e-15 (more than 0.02724 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (2.7433583533677364e-11, 2.7433583533677365e-11). The step is larger than the 2^-28 used at state 60 because the same rule, the largest power of two not above |q1|/(2(|q2|+|q3|+|q4|)), is applied to a derivative about 3.46 times larger. The calculation was made on 2026-10-26, between 06:04Z and 06:08Z UTC, on state 88 as stored at the cut, and changes no accepted state. The scope is the same as for states 6, 8, 10, 16 and 60: state 88 is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on the set that holds its saved defects fixed, and nothing follows for an exactly Taylor-feasible point, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes, or a PDE solution. The comparison state is not an accepted iterate and was not submitted to the optimizer. A separate exact calculation treats an exactly Taylor-feasible point. Changing nine branch-zero coefficients (k = 3 or 4) of the exact reading of state 88, each by less than 1.629e-16 (the Taylor jets by less than 2.81e-13), gives a separate rational potential, with c_l, a_0 and the other 851 coefficients unchanged, in which the center relation and all five Taylor conditions hold exactly. Its whole-axis J_abs exceeds that of state 88 by an exact amount strictly between 2.465560474790166e-21 and 2.465560474790167e-21 and lies inside (2.7441060961075786e-11, 2.7441060961075787e-11). Along the same coordinate, which leaves the center relation, all five Taylor conditions, c_l and a_0 exactly unchanged, the derivative of J_abs at this reference lies strictly between -5.339490302563110e-7 and -5.339490302563109e-7, so the reference is not a stationary point on the exact center-and-five-Taylor set. A step of +2^-26, given by the same rule, lowers J_abs by more than 7.477425022459133e-15 (more than 0.02724 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (2.7433583536053327e-11, 2.7433583536053328e-11). Along this step ||E_1|| increases by more than 2.064e-8 while ||E_2|| and ||E_3|| decrease, so the certified decrease of J_abs does not imply a decrease of the leading far-field defect; the coefficient of sin(39 theta) in E_1 is exactly unchanged and nonzero, so the unnormalized density residual of the reference and of the comparison still grows at infinity. These calculations were made on 2026-10-26 between 06:08Z and 06:31Z UTC on state 88 as stored at the cut. Neither the reference nor the comparison is an accepted state, and neither was submitted to the optimizer.
- Exactly Taylor-feasible reference of accepted state 18 (section 8; verified, exact). Along the same coordinate, the generator r^10 (1 + r^2)^(p_0 - 5), which leaves the center relation, all five Taylor equations, c_l and a_0 exactly unchanged, the whole-axis J_abs of the reference is an exact quartic in the step whose first coefficient lies strictly between -1.055063464078397e-5 and -1.055063464078396e-5. A step of +2^-22 lowers it by more than 2.338052189681077e-12 (about 0.731 percent), to an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 inside (3.1731307283983885e-10, 3.1731307283983886e-10). The whole line lies in the exact center-and-five-Taylor set, so this reference is not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on that set, nor on its slice with c_l and a_0 fixed. On the whole line, over all real steps, this quartic has a unique global minimum, at a step strictly between 1.6902109562498638677e-6 and 1.6902109562498638678e-6, with value strictly between 3.1073474695102393e-10 and 3.1073474695102394e-10, 2.7894092591329253 to 2.7894092591329254 percent below the J_abs of the reference (not of the saved checkpoint); the quartic is not convex on the whole line, and the uniqueness follows from exact sign bounds on its derivative. The E_1 coefficient of sin(39 theta) is the same at every point of the line, so the density residual still grows. Nothing follows for the saved checkpoint, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes or a PDE solution, and neither the minimizer on this line nor the comparison point was given to the optimizer. At that line minimum, the derivative of J_abs along a second generator, r^12 (1 + r^2)^(p_0 - 6) of branch 0 and mode 0, which also leaves the center relation, all five Taylor equations, c_l and a_0 exactly unchanged, lies by exact rational enclosures strictly between 1.267255086372038e-6 and 1.267255086372039e-6, so it is positive, and a small enough step against this generator strictly lowers J_abs inside the exact center-and-five-Taylor set. The line minimizer is therefore not a stationary point of the whole-axis objective on that set. No size of step is claimed from this derivative, nothing follows for the saved checkpoint, the training-mesh objective or a PDE solution, and neither the line minimizer nor the reference is an optimizer state. Taking the rational midpoint of the certified root bracket, strictly between 1.6902109562498638677e-6 and 1.6902109562498638678e-6, as the step along r^10 (1 + r^2)^(p_0 - 5), and adding a step strictly between -2.368057692825779e-7 and -2.368057692825778e-7 along the generator r^12 (1 + r^2)^(p_0 - 6) of branch 0 and mode 0, chosen to minimize only the quadratic part in that step of the exact quartic, gives a rational comparison point in the exact center-and-five-Taylor set, with c_l, a_0 and the other 858 coefficients unchanged, whose J_abs, with all mixed, cubic and quartic terms retained, lies in an exact enclosure of width 1e-145 strictly between 3.105847003882607e-10 and 3.105847003882608e-10, more than 1.500465627631594e-13 (more than 0.04828766793396582 percent) below the line-minimum value, and therefore below J_abs at every point of that line; its E_1 coefficient of sin(39 theta) is unchanged, strictly between -7.387790313591218e-7 and -7.387790313591217e-7, so the density residual still grows; this point is not an optimizer state and is neither a stationary point nor a minimizer on the plane, and nothing follows for the saved checkpoint, the training-mesh objective that the optimizer minimizes or a PDE solution.
- The exactly Taylor-feasible rational reference of section 4 is not stationary either. Nor is the minimum of J_abs along the exact feasible line through it: an exactly feasible curve from that minimum strictly decreases J_abs (section 4, exact feasible descent). Nor is the minimum along the second curve with t fixed at the rational midpoint t0 of the enclosure of alpha: changing the line parameter from it strictly decreases J_abs on the same exactly feasible surface.

Exact profiles inside the finite families (verified: independent proof review and exact identity checks):
- No exact profile with finite angular support. Consider any potential chi = sum_(m=0)^M Re(x+iy)^(2m) v_m(x^2+y^2) with finitely many modes, each v_m of class C^4 on the open half-line q > 0 (no condition at q = 0 is assumed), chi bounded near the origin, and chi = o(r^4) uniformly in the angle. If such a chi satisfies (c_l X + U) . grad rho = rho at every point other than the origin, for some real c_l, then chi = A + B(x^2+y^2) + C(x^2-y^2), and its density and velocity vanish. This holds for every number M of modes and covers in particular smooth or real-analytic radial functions on [0, infinity).
  - Proof idea: the highest angular harmonic sin((2n+3) theta) of the transport term receives only the top modes of psi and rho. With n = 2M for the highest nonzero index M, its vanishing forces w = v_M'' to solve (n+2) w (q w'' + 2 w') - (n+1) q (w')^2 = 0. On each interval where w is nonzero this gives |w|^(1/(n+2)) = A + B/q. On an interval that reaches the origin, B != 0 would make the angular coefficient q^M v_M of the potential unbounded near the origin (like log q for M = 0 and like q^(-M) for M >= 1), so B = 0 there. An interval with a finite left endpoint a carries w = C (1 - a/q)^(n+2), and no interval has two finite positive endpoints. Hence if w is not identically zero it tends to a nonzero constant at infinity, and v_M grows like q^2, against subquartic growth. So w = 0, v_M is affine, and growth leaves only the displayed zero-field kernel. No power series or analyticity is used.
  - Consequence for this campaign: the radial functions of the 860 generators are smooth on [0, infinity), so the potential is bounded near the origin, and every generator grows like r^(2 p_j) with 2 p_j <= 3 + 1/c_l < 4. So no coefficient vector of the 860 family, at any c_l > 1, is an exact profile with nonzero central density slope. The same holds for any enlargement that keeps finitely many angular modes of this form, C^4 radial functions away from the origin, a potential bounded near the origin and this growth.
  - Within this class, a nonzero exact profile must fail finite angular support, boundedness near the origin or subquartic growth. The regularity C^4 is what the argument uses, since the transport equation contains fourth derivatives of chi; it is not claimed optimal, and weak or distributional solutions are outside the theorem. For n >= 2 the reduced radial equation does have bounded nonconstant glued solutions w of class C^(n+1); they are excluded by the growth condition, not by regularity. Without boundedness near the origin the conclusion fails: chi = log(x^2+y^2) and, for each m >= 1, chi = Re((x+iy)^(-2m)) are harmonic away from the origin, so rho = 0 and the transport equation holds there for every c_l, and they are subquartic, but their velocity is not zero. These examples have zero density, so they do not show that boundedness is needed for the exclusion of nonzero density. The example chi = -a (x^2+y^2)^2 / 32 solves the transport equation at c_l = 1 + a/4 with density a y, which shows that the growth condition cannot be dropped.
  - Arbitrary finite angular support (verified: independent proof review and exact identity checks). For chi = v_0(q) + sum_(n=1)^L Re((x+iy)^n v_n(q)) with finitely many modes of any parity and of sine or cosine phase, real v_0 and complex v_n of class C^4 on q > 0, chi bounded near the origin and chi = o(r^4) uniformly in the angle, every classical solution of (c_l X + U) . grad rho = rho on the punctured plane, for a real constant c_l, has a potential that is a real polynomial of degree at most three in an explicit nine-dimensional space, zero density and a constant velocity that need not vanish (chi = x^3 - 3xy^2 gives U = (6, 0)).
- An earlier, weaker form of the same conclusion separates the eight radial exponent classes of the residual. It holds for c_l outside {7/5, 3/2, 5/3, 2, 7/3, 3, 5, 7}, an excluded set that does not meet the interval (5/3, 2) containing the accepted c_l.
- Limits:
  - Neither statement bounds J or any residual norm from below.
  - Neither excludes further improvement of the residual by optimization or by larger families.
  - Neither says anything about profiles with infinitely many angular modes or about weak solutions.
  - A small objective inside these families is therefore always an approximation, never an exact solution.

Exploratory comparison family with growing radial degree (verified: independent proofs, a named proof review of each proof, including the finite-K axis and off-axis enclosures, and independent numerical checks). These potentials lie outside the 860 family, so none of this is a campaign result.
- Construction. Take the exactly Taylor-feasible rational reference of section 4 and add h_K = r^(2K) (1 + r^2)^(1 + gamma - K) H(theta), gamma = 1/c_l. Here H is the even cosine polynomial whose twenty coefficients are the branch-1 moment changes of the complete E_1 cancellation in the twenty-mode extension above. For every integer K >= 25 the center relation, the five Taylor conditions and a_0 are unchanged, and E_1 = 0 exactly. Each such state therefore has a density residual that tends to zero at infinity.
- Objective cost. With lambda = 1 - 1/c_l, J_abs(chi_K) - J_abs(reference) = O(K^(-2 lambda)), and the quotient-residual distance to the reference is O(K^(-lambda)).
  - K^(2 lambda) times the objective difference tends to the closed-form constant Gamma(2 lambda)/(8 A^2) [2L + 2^(-2 lambda) M], approximately 3.88621596193392608. Here L and M are finite rational sums and A is the fixed normalization of J_abs.
  - A direct quadrature of the limit integrals reproduces this constant to all printed digits.
  - A full-field evaluation for K = 25 x 4^n up to 4.19e8 approaches the constant non-monotonically: 14.22 at K = 25, a minimum of about 3.858 near K = 3e7 to 1e8, and 3.862 at K = 4.19e8. A fit in powers of K^(-lambda/2) on the last six values gives 3.8862151.
  - The approach is slow. At K = 25, J_abs is 0.799, about 1.9e8 times the reference value.
- Physical residual. The unnormalized density residual D_K = y R_K of the same states satisfies K^(-delta) D_K(sqrt(K) q, theta) -> q^(1 - 2 lambda) sin(theta) [e(theta) + exp(-1/q^2) Q(1/q^2, theta)], with delta = 1/c_l - 1/2, approximately 0.0528. Here e is the leading far-field coefficient of the reference quotient residual and Q is an explicit polynomial in its first argument.
  - The limit is not identically zero. Each sup |D_K| over the plane is finite, but these maxima diverge at least like a positive constant times K^delta.
  - At the point (0, sqrt(K)) the limit is 2.2646, with an exact rational enclosure that excludes zero.
  - Along the same point, |D_K(0, sqrt(K))| is 10.29 at K = 25, falls to 5.36 at K = 4.19e8 and rises again to 9.441433818 at K = 1e12. The last value is enclosed by an exact rational interval calculation at that finite K, and an independent high-precision evaluation agrees to all printed digits. These are values at one sampled point. They establish no threshold or monotonicity, and they do not locate the maxima of |D_K|.
  - The convergence above is uniform on the whole plane. Hence K^(-delta) sup |D_K| tends to M = sup |G|, where G is the limit profile on the right-hand side above. For all sufficiently large K, every maximizer of |D_K| lies at a radius between a sqrt(K) and b sqrt(K), for fixed constants 0 < a < b.
  - An exact rational interval calculation of G at an explicit point gives M > 33.5039122832. An exact covering of the plane gives M < 34: a binary subdivision of a bounded range of z = 1/q^2 and of all angles, with Bernstein enclosures in the angular variable and rational interval arithmetic, together with explicit bounds on the two remaining ranges of z. Its 5,547 leaf bounds were recomputed by a separate reader. Hence 33.5039122832 < M < 34; the earlier coefficient bound gave only M < 724.3066756526. On both coordinate axes an exact coefficient bound gives |G| < 12.1005, below that point value. Every maximizer of the limit |G| therefore lies off both axes, and for all sufficiently large K so does every maximizer of |D_K|; the proof gives no explicit threshold in K. A numerical search puts the largest |G| at about 33.504, near theta = 0.159 pi and q = 0.683. The search does not prove that this is the global maximum or that it is unique, and no finite-K upper bound is proved.
  - At the explicit off-axis point, the exact intervals certify sup |D_K| > 59.81, 61.20, 73.60, 96.34 and 144.28 for K = 25, 6,400, 1,638,400, 419,430,400 and 10^12 respectively. An independent 180-digit Cartesian reconstruction lies inside each exact interval. These are statewise lower bounds, not maxima or monotonicity.
- Meaning. In growing radial spaces, cancelling E_1 can be made nearly free in J_abs, but it moves the physical residual outward to radii of order sqrt(K) instead of removing it. E_1 is not continuous in the residual norm that defines J_abs. A small J_abs together with E_1 = 0 is therefore not evidence of an exact profile either.
- Limits. These statements concern one prescribed correction in growing radial spaces. They do not identify any constrained optimum. They give no bound for all members of the primary 860 family or for its constrained optimum; the comparison states use angular modes through 19 and are not optimizer states.

Next step:
- The leading far-field density coefficient is E_1 = -(c_l - 1) F_1 + N_00. F_1 depends linearly on the ten leading branch-1 moments C_(m,1) = sum_k a_(m,k,1), m = 0..9, and N_00 quadratically on the branch-0 moments. The map from these moments to the sine harmonics 1, 3, ..., 19 of F_1 is upper bidiagonal. Its diagonal entries are -8 gamma (gamma + 1)^2 for m = 0 and -4 (gamma - m)(gamma - m + 1)(gamma + m + 1) for m = 1..9, with gamma = 1/c_l, none of which vanishes for c_l > 1. (Verified: independent proof, independent proof review and two exact computations.)
- So at fixed c_l and fixed branch 0 exactly one choice of the ten moments cancels these ten harmonics. It can be realized by changing only the ten coefficients a_(m,max(m,5),1), which leaves the center relation, the five Taylor conditions and a_0 unchanged. With the ten extra conditions the feasible set is, near each of its points with a_0 != 0, a smooth manifold of dimension 845, with c_l and the amplitude still free.
- The remaining harmonics 21 to 39 (0.1446% of the baseline E_1 norm, section 8) come from branch 0 alone. In the m <= 9 family they vanish only if C_(m,0) = 0 for m = 5..9, so the growth r^(2/c_l - 1) cannot be removed by the ten branch-1 conditions alone.
- Extending branch 1 to modes m <= 19 (the same k <= 25) gives a twenty-by-twenty map with the same structure, still invertible, and allows E_1 = 0 exactly. The next possible growth exponent of the density residual is then 3/c_l - 2, which is negative for c_l > 3/2 (-0.342 at the baseline c_l). That term can itself vanish, so this is the next possible exponent, not a proved rate.
- Costs and open points. At the baseline c_l the 2-norm condition number of the map is 3.24e4 for ten modes and 1.02e6 for twenty (106.5 and 326.2 after scaling each column to unit norm). The conditions do not preserve the residual at finite radii. Whether a small J_abs survives them is unknown. Cancelling the next coefficients E_2 and E_3 is not a simple extension: the corresponding density maps are singular at c_l = 3 and c_l = 2 respectively, and at c_l = 3 a radial correction of branch 0 enters at the exponent of E_3.
- Two explicit corrections of the exactly Taylor-feasible rational reference of section 4 (J_abs = 4.2683726070954721e-9) show the effect of imposing these conditions through one prescribed choice of coefficients, with every other coefficient, c_l and a_0 fixed.
  - Ten modes: change only a_(m,max(m,5),1) for m = 0..9 so that the ten represented harmonics of E_1 vanish. J_abs becomes 3.7842295933, about 8.87e8 times the reference. E_1 is then exactly its unchanged harmonics 21 to 39 (L2 norm 6.3692e-4), so the density residual still grows like r^(2/c_l - 1). The other norms are ||E_2|| = 12.502 and ||E_3|| = 30.416.
  - Twenty modes: in the extension of branch 1 to m <= 19, change a_(m,max(m,5),1) for m = 0..19. E_1 then vanishes completely, J_abs is 3.7702976814, ||E_2|| = 12.502 and ||E_3|| = 30.416.
  - Both objective values are exact rational differences added to the certified reference value. Each was confirmed by an independent reconstruction and by a separate quadrature.
  - The corrections change the interior substantially: the largest coefficient changes are about -0.94 and -1.13, at m = 0 and m = 1. Each describes one explicit state for one distribution over k. Minimizing J_abs over the remaining directions that keep the conditions is still open, and these values give no lower bound for it.
- This branch changes the problem and is not comparable with the fixed-objective campaign of sections 7 to 9. It would be run separately and report E_1, E_2, E_3 and J_abs for every state.
